H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\simeq(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}H^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}).

In particular, the equivariant cohomology ring
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
is a free
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module.
Moreover, in this case the ordinary cohomology ring
H^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
is determined by the
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-algebra structure of
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
via the isomorphism

(64)

H^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})/{I_{\mathbb{T}}H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})},

where
I_{\mathbb{T}}\subset(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
is the ideal generated by elements of positive degree, as in
[
13
]
[Prop.3.2] or
[
16
]
[Cor.12.4.8]. (In particular, the quotient
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{I_{\mathbb{T}}}\simeq{\mathbb{Q}}
gives
{\mathbb{Q}}
the structure of a
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module.)

For the remaining of this Subsection
2.5
, assume moreover that
X
is a
complete simplicial
toric variety.

There are several different descriptions of the rational equivariant cohomology
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
of a complete simplicial toric variety
X=X_{\Sigma}
associated to the fan
\Sigma
in
N\otimes\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}
, e.g., see
[
11
]
,
[
16
]
[Sect.12.4]
or
[
28
]
[Lecture 13]. Such descriptions, which are recalled below, are related to various aspects of the Euler-Maclaurin formulae for simple lattice polytopes (see Sections
5
and
7
below):

(a)

H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
can be realized as the
Stanley-Reisner ring

SR_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(\Sigma)
of the fan
\Sigma
(see, e.g.,
[
16
]
[Sect.12.4]), i.e.,

SR_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(\Sigma):=\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]/\sim_{SR}\>\>\simeq H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})

x_{i}\longmapsto[D_{i}]_{\mathbb{T}}=c^{1}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{O}(D_{i}))\>,

with
x_{i}
of degree two and
D_{i}
the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor corresponding to the ray
\rho_{i}=\langle u_{i}\rangle\in\Sigma(1)
with ray generator
u_{i}

(
i=1,\dots,r
), and
the Stanley-Reisner relation
\sim_{SR}
(or, in the notations of Subsection
2.1.1
, the ideal
\mathcal{I}
) generated by:

x_{i_{1}}\cdots x_{i_{s}}=0

for distinct
i_{j}
with
\{\rho_{i_{j}}|j=1,\dots,s\}
not spanning a cone of
\Sigma
.

(b)
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
can be realized as the ring
R_{\Sigma}
of continuous piecewise polynomial functions (aka, polynomial splines) with rational coefficients on the fan
\Sigma
(see, e.g.,
[
13
]
[Prop.3.2] or
[
16
]
[page 606]). This can be seen by starting with the following exact sequence in equivariant cohomology (as appearing in the proof of
[
16
]
[Lem.12.4.17]):

(65)

0\to H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\to\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma};\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\to\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma(n-1)}H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\tau};\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\>,

where
U_{\sigma}\subset X_{\Sigma}
denotes as usual the unique
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant open affine subset containing the corresponding
{\mathbb{T}}
-orbit

O_{\sigma}
, and
O_{\sigma}=x_{\sigma}\in X
are the
{\mathbb{T}}
-fixed points in the case
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
(i.e.,
\sigma
is of top dimension

n
). Note also that
x_{\sigma}
corresponds to a vertex of the lattice polytope
P
in the case when
\Sigma=\Sigma_{P}
is the corresponding inner normal fan. There are equivariant deformation retracts
U_{\sigma}\to O_{\sigma}
for any
\sigma\in\Sigma
(e.g., see
[
16
]
[Prop.12.1.9(a)]), hence

H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma};\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\simeq H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(O_{\sigma};\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}).

Moreover, for
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
one gets by (
61
):

H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(O_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq Sym_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(M),

and for
\tau\in\Sigma(n-1)
one has an isomorphism

H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(O_{\tau};{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq Sym_{\mathbb{Q}}(M_{\tau}),

for
M_{\tau}=M/{\tau^{\perp}\cap M}
.
So the localization sequence (
65
) translates into the exact sequence

(66)

0\to H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\to\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M)\to\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma(n-1)}Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M_{\tau})\>,

with
Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M)
, resp.,
Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M_{\tau})
the ring of polynomial functions with rational coefficients on
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
, resp.,

\tau\in\Sigma(n-1)
. In other words, an element of
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
can be thought of as a collection
\{f_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}
, where
f_{\sigma}
is a polynomial function on
\sigma
, such that
f_{\sigma}|_{\tau}=f_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{\tau}
whenever
\sigma\cap\sigma^{\prime}=\tau\in\Sigma(n-1)
.

(c)
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
can also be described as the
H_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})=(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}
-algebra

(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]/\sim\>\>\simeq H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})

x_{i}\longmapsto[D_{i}]_{\mathbb{T}}=c^{1}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{O}(D_{i})),

and
\sim
generated by the Stanley-Reisner relation
\sim_{SR}
as well as

(67)

\pm m:=c(m)=-s(m)=c^{1}_{\mathbb{T}}({\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m}})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\;\langle m,u_{i}\rangle x_{i}\quad\text{for all $m\in M$}\>.

Here we consider the
+
-sign in the case when we use the isomorphism

c:Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M)\simeq H_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})=:(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}

coming from
c^{1}_{\mathbb{T}}
as usual in algebraic geometry (see
[
28
]
[Lecture 13]).
But the use of the minus-sign and the isomorphism

s:Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M)\simeq H_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})=:(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}

induced from
c^{1}_{\mathbb{T}}\circ(-)^{\vee}
(e.g., as in
[
16
]
[Sect.12.4]) fits better with the corresponding Euler-Maclaurin formulae and the equivariant Riemann-Roch theorem (e.g., see
[
16
]
[Sect.13.3]).

Remark 2.7
.

The inclusion
\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]\subset(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]
induces the natural map

\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]/\sim_{SR}\ \ \longrightarrow(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]/\sim\>\>,

which is an isomorphism relating parts (a) and (c) above, see Lemma
7.1
and compare also with
[
28
]
[Lecture13, Lem.2.2 and Rem.2.7]. Note that in (a) and (b) the
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}
-algebra structure of

H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
is not explicitly mentioned, but follows then from relation (
67
)
(compare also with
[
16
]
[Sect.12.4]).

In the context of a complete simplicial toric variety
X=X_{\Sigma}
there is also no difference between the rational equivariant (co)homology ring and the corresponding rational equivariant Chow ring of
X
(see
[
11
]
[Sect.5]).

Finally note that by (
64
) one has that
c^{1}_{\mathbb{T}}({\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m}})\mapsto 0
under the natural map
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\to H^{*}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
obtained by forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action,
so the relation (
67
) maps to the corresponding additional relation in the description (
30
) of
H^{*}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
as a quotient
of the Stanley-Reisner ring.

As it shall be indicated below, the equivariant Chern character
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{E})
of a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant line or vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
on
X
,
as well as the image of the equivariant Todd homology class
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\in\widehat{H}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq{\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
under equivariant Poincaré duality,
live in the completion

\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}):=\prod_{i\geq 0}\>H^{i}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}),

with

(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}:=\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\simeq\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}[[t_{1},\dots,t_{n}]]\>.

Under the isomorphisms described in this subsection, this completion corresponds to:

(a)

\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}[[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]]/\sim_{SR}\>\>\simeq\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
.

(b)

the corresponding completion
\widehat{R}_{\Sigma}\simeq\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
of the ring of continuous
piecewise polynomial functions with rational coefficients on the fan
\Sigma
.

(c)

(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}[[x_{1},\dots,x_{r}]]/\sim\>\>\simeq\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
.

Abstractly, these are completions of connected integer graded commutative rings
R^{*}
with respect to the maximal ideal
R^{>0}
given by positive degree elements.

In fact, as we will see
and use later on, the equivariant Chern character and Todd homology classes live in an
analytic subring

(H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}))^{an}\subset\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})

(see (
194
)),
with
\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}\{t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\}\simeq(H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}))^{an}=:(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}
the subring of
convergent power series
(around zero) with rational coefficients, i.e., after pairing with
z\in N_{\mbox{$\mathbb{K}$}}=N\otimes_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}}\mbox{$\mathbb{K}$}
(for
\mbox{$\mathbb{K}$}=\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$},\mbox{$\mathbb{C}$}
) one gets a convergent power series
function
in
z
around zero, whose corresponding Taylor polynomials have rational coefficients.

Remark 2.8
.

The injectivity part of the sequence (
65
) still holds (as in
[
16
]
[Cor.12.4.9]) even if
X
is only a simplicial toric variety with a fan of full-dimensional convex support, since the equivariant cohomology of
X
is a free
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module.

2.6.
Equivariant Euler characteristic

Assume that a torus
{\mathbb{T}}
with character lattice
M
acts linearly on a finite dimensional complex vector space
W
, with eigenspace

(68)

W_{\chi^{m}}:=\{w\in W\mid t\cdot w=\chi^{m}(t)w\ \text{ for all}\ t\in{\mathbb{T}}\},

for
\chi^{m}
a character of
T
. Then, as in
[
16
]
[Prop.1.1.2], one has an
eigenspace
decomposition

W=\bigoplus_{m\in M}W_{\chi^{m}}.

This induces an isomorphism

(69)

K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M],\ \ [W]\mapsto\sum_{m\in M}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}W_{\chi^{m}}\cdot\chi^{m}.

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be a complete toric variety of dimension
n
, with a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
. The cohomology spaces
H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{F}})
are finite dimensional
{\mathbb{T}}
-representations, vanishing for
i
large enough. We define the
K
-theoretic Euler characteristic
of
{\mathcal{F}}
as:

(70)

\begin{split}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})&:=[H^{*}(X;{\mathcal{F}})]:=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}[H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{F}})]\in K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt)\\
&=\sum_{m\in M}\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{m}}\cdot\chi^{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M].\end{split}

By applying the equivariant Chern character
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}:{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\simeq K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt)\hookrightarrow(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}
, we further get the
cohomological Euler characteristic
of
{\mathcal{F}}
:

(71)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})=\sum_{m\in M}\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{m}}\cdot e^{c(m)}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\>.

Let
D
be a torus-invariant Cartier divisor on
X
. For each
i\geq 0
, consider the
weight
decomposition of
H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))
given by (e.g., see
[
16
]
[Sect.9.1]):

(72)

H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\bigoplus_{m\in M}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{m},

induced via a C̆ech resolution from

(73)

H^{0}(U_{\sigma};{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\bigoplus_{m}H^{0}(U_{\sigma};{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{m}\subset{\mathbb{C}}[M],

where each
H^{0}(U_{\sigma};{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{m}
is either
0
or
{\mathbb{C}}\cdot\chi^{m}
.

For comparing this weight decomposition with the eigenspace decomposition of (
68
), we recall here that in this paper the torus
{\mathbb{T}}
acts on
{\mathbb{C}}[M]
as follows: if
t\in{\mathbb{T}}
and
f\in{\mathbb{C}}[M]
, then
t\cdot f\in{\mathbb{C}}[M]
is given by
p\mapsto f(t^{-1}\cdot p)
, for
p\in{\mathbb{T}}
(see
[
16
]
[pag.18]). In particular,
t\cdot\chi^{m}=\chi^{m}(t^{-1})\chi^{m}
, so that

H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{m}=H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{\chi^{-m}}.

So, via
s(m)=c(-m)=-c_{1}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m}})
, formula (
71
) translates into
the
equivariant Euler characteristic
of
D
, as defined in
[
16
]
[Def.13.3.2]:

(74)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\sum_{m\in M}\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\dim H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))_{m}\cdot e^{s(m)}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\>.

Note that
i^{*}_{pt}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\chi(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))
, where
i^{*}_{pt}:(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}
sends elements of positive degree to zero (i.e., one forgets the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action).

Example 2.9
.

In the special case when
X_{P}
is the projective toric variety associated to a full-dimensional lattice polytope
P\subset M_{{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, and
D_{P}
is the corresponding ample Cartier divisor, then the formulae (
38
)–(
41
) yield

(75)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{P},{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D_{P}))=\sum_{m\in P\cap M}e^{s(m)}

and

(76)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{P},{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(-D_{P}))=(-1)^{n}\sum_{m\in{\rm Int}(P)\cap M}e^{s(m)}.

∎

Example 2.10
.

For later applications, let us also consider the following more general context.
Let
X
be a toric variety and
D
a torus-invariant Cartier divisor. Then

(77)

\Gamma(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\bigoplus_{m\in P_{D}\cap M}{\mathbb{C}}\cdot\chi^{m}\subset{\mathbb{C}}[M],

where
\chi^{m}
denotes the character defined by
m\in M
, and
P_{D}
is the polyhedron associated to the divisor
D
(see
[
16
]
[Prop.4.3.3]). If, moreover, the fan of
X
has full-dimensional convex support and
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
is globally generated, then
P_{D}
is a
lattice
polyhedron (see
[
16
]
[Thm.6.1.7]), and

(78)

H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=0,\ {\rm for\ all}\ i>0

(e.g., see
[
16
]
[Prop.9.2.3]). Hence, for
X
complete and
D
as above (with
P_{D}
the corresponding lattice polytope), we get

(79)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\sum_{m\in P_{D}\cap M}e^{s(m)}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}.

∎

2.7.
Equivariant Chern character and equivariant Riemann-Roch map.

Let
X
be a complex algebraic variety with an algebraic action of the torus
{\mathbb{T}}
with character lattice
M
(e.g.,
X
is a toric variety). For any
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}\to X
, one can define equivariant Chern classes
c_{i}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{E}})
, equivariant Todd classes
{\rm Td}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{E}})
and an equivariant Chern character
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{E}})
in
\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}),
by means of the corresponding Chern roots, see
[
23
]
[Sect.3.1] for complete details. Forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action, these notions reduce under
i_{X}^{*}:\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\to{H}^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
to the classical non-equivariant counterparts.
For example, if
D
is a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor on
X
, then the equivariant Chern character of the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
is given by:

(80)

{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=e^{[D]_{\mathbb{T}}}=1+[D]_{\mathbb{T}}+\frac{1}{2!}[D]_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}+\cdots\in\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}),

where
[D]_{\mathbb{T}}=c_{1}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))\in H^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
is the equivariant cohomology class of the torus-invariant Cartier divisor
D
. Forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action yields:
i_{X}^{*}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))={\rm ch}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)).

We denote as usual by
K_{\mathbb{T}}^{0}(X)
the Grothendieck group of
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant vector bundles on
X
, and we let
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
denote the Grothendieck group of
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaves on
X
. If
X
is smooth and quasi-projective, then
K_{\mathbb{T}}^{0}(X)\simeq K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)
, since in this case any
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf has a finite resolution by
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant locally free sheaves.
In general, the tensor product gives a ring structure on
K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)
, and
K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)
is a module for this ring.
Note that the Chern character
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}
induces a contravariant, functorial, ring homomorphism

{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}:K_{\mathbb{T}}^{0}(X)\longrightarrow\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{T}}^{2*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}).

Edidin-Graham defined in
[
24
]
[Thm.3.1] an
equivariant Riemann-Roch map
, which in our setup can be given as

{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}:K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)\longrightarrow{\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow\widehat{H}^{\mathbb{T}}_{2*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}),

with the same functoriality as in the nonequivariant case of Baum-Fulton-MacPherson
[
4
]
.
Here, we use the completions

{\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}:=\prod_{i\leq\dim(X)}{CH}_{i}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}):=\prod_{i\leq\dim(X)}\widehat{H}_{i}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}).

(These equivariant Chow and Borel-Moore homology groups can be non-zero also in negative degrees.) Compare also with
[
15
]
for the homological version of this transformation.

The transformation
{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}
has the following properties:

(a)

(functoriality)
{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}
is covariant for
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant proper morphisms;

(b)

(module property) if
\beta\in K_{\mathbb{T}}^{0}(X)
and
\alpha\in K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
, then:

(81)

{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(\beta\otimes\alpha)={\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}(\beta)\cap{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(\alpha).

Forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action, one recovers under the forgetful map
\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\to CH_{*}(X)
the classical nonequivariant Todd class transformation of Baum-Fulton-MacPherson
[
4
]
.

The
equivariant Todd class
of
X
is defined as:

{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X):={\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}]).

If
X
is smooth, one also has the
normalization property
(see
[
2
]
[Sect.6.1]):

(82)

{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X)={\rm Td}^{\mathbb{T}}(T_{X})\cap[X]_{\mathbb{T}},

with
{\rm Td}^{\mathbb{T}}(T_{X})
the equivariant cohomological Todd class of the tangent bundle
T_{X}
of
X
.

Example 2.11
.

Assume
X=pt
is a point space. By the normalization property,
{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(pt)=[pt].
So by the module property, one gets via the equivariant Poincaré duality that
{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}:K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(pt)\to\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})
reduces to the equivariant Chern character, resp., Euler characteristic map

{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}=\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(pt,-):{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\simeq K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt)\hookrightarrow(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}.

∎

For a compact variety
X
, using the functoriality and module properties of
{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}
for the constant map
f:X\to pt
, one gets the following equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch formula (see
[
24
]
[Cor.3.1]):

Theorem 2.12

(Equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch)
.

Let
X
be a compact complex algebraic variety with an algebraic action of the torus
{\mathbb{T}}
, and let
{\mathcal{E}}
, resp.,
{\mathcal{F}}
, be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant vector bundle, resp., coherent sheaf on
X
. Then

(83)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{E}}\otimes{\mathcal{F}})=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{E}})\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([{\mathcal{F}}]),

where
\int_{X}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})=(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is the equivariant Gysin map (or, equivalently, the equivariant pushforward) for the constant map
X\to pt
.

Example
2.9
and Theorem
2.12
yield the following:

Corollary 2.13
.

Let
P\subset M_{{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a full-dimensional lattice polytope, with corresponding projective toric variety
X=X_{P}
and ample Cartier divisor
D=D_{P}
. Then:

(84)

\sum_{m\in P\cap M}e^{s(m)}=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X),

(85)

\sum_{m\in{\rm Int}(P)\cap M}e^{s(m)}=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}).

Proof.

Formula (
84
) follows directly from (
75
) and (
83
). For (
85
) we proceed as in (
42
) and (
46
), using the fact that toric Serre duality holds equivariantly.
∎

Similarly, Example
2.10
and Theorem
2.12
yield:

Corollary 2.14
.

Let
X
be a complete toric variety, and
D
a globally generated torus-invariant Cartier divisor with associated lattice polytope
P_{D}\subset M_{{\mathbb{R}}}
. Then

(86)

\sum_{m\in P_{D}\cap M}e^{s(m)}=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X).

3.
Equivariant characteristic classes of toric varieties.

In this section, we first extend in Subection
3.1
the characteristic class formulae from
[
36
,
37
]
for the motivic Chern and Hirzebruch classes of a toric variety to the equivariant setting. Furthermore, we calculate the pushforward of these classes under toric fibrations, using the motivic nature of these characteristic classes. This will be used in Subsection
3.2
for applying the generalized equivariant Hirzerbruch-Riemann-Roch formula in the context of a globally generated
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor.
In Subsection
3.3
,
we use the global Cox construction (as reviewed in Subsection
2.1.2
) and the equivariant Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch theorem of Edidin-Graham
[
24
]
for computing various equivariant characteristic classes in a suitable context of simplicial toric varieties, including the classical projective case, as well as the case for the complement of a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor.
Alternative proofs of all these characteristic class formulae will be given in the next section via localization at the torus fixed points.

3.1.
Definition. Properties

In this subsection we recall the definitions of equivariant motivic Chern and Hirzebruch classes of complex quasi-projective varieties with an algebraic
{\mathbb{T}}
-action.

3.1.1.
Equivariant motivic Chern class transformation.

Recall that if
X
is a complex quasi-projective variety with an algebraic
{\mathbb{T}}
-action,
the (relative) equivariant motivic Grothendieck group
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X)
of varieties over
X
is the free abelian group generated by symbols
[Z\to X]
, where
Z
is a quasi-projective
{\mathbb{T}}
-variety and
Z\to X
is a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant morphism, modulo the additivity relation:

[Z\to X]=[U\to X]+[Z\setminus U\to X],

for
U\subset Z
an open
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant subvariety. If
X=pt
is a point space, then
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/pt)
is a ring with product given by the external product of morphisms, and the group
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X)
is a module over
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/pt)
with respect to the external product.

For any equivariant morphism
f:X\to Y
of quasi-projective
{\mathbb{T}}
-varieties, there is a well-defined push-forward
f_{!}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X)\to K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/Y)
defined by composition. One can also define an exterior product:
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X)\boxtimes K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X^{\prime})\to K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X\times X^{\prime})
via the cross-product:
[Z\to X]\times[Z^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}]=[Z\times Z^{\prime}\to X\times X^{\prime}]
.

The following result provides an equivariant analogue of the motivic Chern class transformation of
[
8
]
; see
[
1
]
[Thm.4.2]:

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
X
be a complex quasi-projective variety with an action of the torus
{\mathbb{T}}
. There exists a unique natural transformation

mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X)\longrightarrow K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y]

satisfying the following properties:

(a)

(functoriality)
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
is covariant for
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant proper morphisms;

(b)

(normalization) if
X
is smooth, then

(87)

mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([id_{X}])=\Lambda_{y}(T^{*}_{X}):=\sum_{p=0}^{\dim(X)}[\Lambda^{p}T^{*}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot y^{p}\in K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y]\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y].

Moreover,
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
commutes with exterior products.

As a consequence of these properties,
the transformation
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
is determined by its image on classes
[f:Z\to X]=f_{*}[id_{Z}]
, where
Z
is a
smooth
quasi-projective variety and
f:Z\to X
is a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant
proper
morphism. For such
[f]
, one has:

mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([f:Z\to X])=f_{*}mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([id_{Z}])=f_{*}(\Lambda_{y}(T^{*}_{Z})\otimes[\mathcal{O}_{Z}]).

So the uniqueness of
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
follows from equivariant resolution of singularities.

Definition 3.2
.

The
equivariant motivic Chern class
of a complex quasi-projective variety
X
with an algebraic
{\mathbb{T}}
-action is defined as:

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X):=mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([id_{X}]).

We next discuss a calculation of motivic Chern classes in terms of cubical hyper-resolutions, which will be used for proving a generalized equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch theorem in the toric context.
Choose an equivariant simplicial resolution
f_{\centerdot}:X_{\centerdot}\to X
of
X
derived from a cubical hyper-resolution in the sense of
[
32
]
[Théorème 2.15], with each
X_{i}
a smooth
{\mathbb{T}}
-variety and
f_{i}:X_{i}\to X
a proper
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant morphism (with
\dim(X_{i})\leq\dim(X)-i
). Then

(88)

[id_{X}]=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}[f_{i}:X_{i}\to X]\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(var/X).

Indeed, (
88
) follows from the inductive construction of an equivariant cubical hyper-resolution, by the following abstract equivariant blowup relation. Let
f:\widetilde{X}\to X
be a proper
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant map of complex quasi-projective varieties. Let
D\subset X
be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant subvariety, with
E:=f^{-1}(D)\subset\widetilde{X}
. Assume
f:\widetilde{X}\setminus E\to X\setminus D
is an isomorphism. Then

(89)

[\widetilde{X}\to X]-[E\to X]=[id_{X}]-[D\to X]\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(var/X).

Applying the transformation
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
to (
88
) yields:

(90)

\begin{split}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)&=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([f_{i}:X_{i}\to X])\\
&=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{f_{i}}_{*}(\Lambda_{y}(T^{*}_{X_{i}})\otimes[\mathcal{O}_{X_{i}}])\\
&=\sum_{p\geq 0}\left(\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{f_{i}}_{*}([\Omega^{p}_{X_{i}}]_{\mathbb{T}})\right)\cdot y^{p}.\end{split}

In particular, for any
p\geq 0
, the Grothendieck class

\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{f_{i}}_{*}([\Omega^{p}_{X_{i}}]_{\mathbb{T}})=:[Rf_{\centerdot*}\Omega^{p}_{X_{\centerdot}}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)

is independent of the choice of an equivariant cubical hyper-resolution, and it provides an equivariant analogue of the Grothendieck class

[\ \uuline{\Omega}^{p}_{X}]\in K_{0}(X)
appearing in (
51
), corresponding to the graded pieces of the filtered Du Bois complex of
X
. So, while an equivariant version of the Du Bois complex is not available in the literature, the Grothendieck classes of its graded pieces are well defined equivariantly by the equivariant blow-up relation. This is sufficient for the purpose of this paper.

As an application of the above formula (
90
), Weber
[
44
]
[Thm.5.1, Rem.5.2] deduces the following result:

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective
{\mathbb{T}}
-variety, and
U\subset
X an open subvariety so that
X\setminus U=:D=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}D_{i}
is a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant simple normal crossing divisor. Then:

(91)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([U\hookrightarrow X])=[{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-D)\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log D)]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\in K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y]\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y]\>.

In fact Weber
[
44
]
[Thm.5.1, Rem.5.2] formulated his results in the non-equivariant context, but his proof also works
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariantly as follows. By the inclusion-exclusion formula for the
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant motivic Chern class one has

(92)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([U\hookrightarrow X])=\sum_{I\subset\{1,\dots,m\}}(-1)^{|I|}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([D_{I}\hookrightarrow X])=\sum_{I\subset\{1,\dots,m\}}(-1)^{|I|}[\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{D_{I}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\>,

with
D_{I}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}D_{i}
smooth
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed subvarieties (and
D_{\emptyset}:=X
). Similarly

[{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-D)]_{{\mathbb{T}}}=\sum_{I\subset\{1,\dots,m\}}(-1)^{|I|}[{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{I}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\in K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\>.

Then Deligne’s weight filtration of the sheaf of logarithmic
p
-forms
\Omega^{p}_{X}(\log D)=\Lambda^{p}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log D)
is also
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant
(and similarly for the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant normal crossing divisor
D_{>I}:=\bigcup_{I\subset J}D_{J}

\subset D_{I}
), so that the calculation of Weber
[
44
]
[Thm.5.1, Rem.5.2] holds
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariantly:

(93)

\sum_{I\subset\{1,\dots,m\}}(-1)^{|I|}[\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{D_{I}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-D)\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log D)]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\in K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y]\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y]\>.

3.1.2.
Equivariant Hirzebruch class transformation.

Let
X
be a complex quasi-projective variety
X
with an algebraic
{\mathbb{T}}
-action. An equivariant version of the Hirzebruch class transformation of
[
8
]
can be defined as follows (see also
[
2
,
44
]
).

Definition 3.4
.

The un-normalized
equivariant Hirzebruch class
transformation

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}
is the composition:

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}:={\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}\circ mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(var/X)\longrightarrow{\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}[y]\longrightarrow\widehat{H}^{\mathbb{T}}_{2*}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}[y],

while a normalized version
\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}
is obtained by precomposing
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}
with the normalization functor

\Psi_{(1+y)}:{\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}[y]\to{\widehat{CH}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}[y,(1+y)^{-1}]):=\prod_{i\leq\dim(X)}{CH}_{i}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}[y,(1+y)^{-1}]

given in degree
k
by multiplication by
(1+y)^{-k}
.

By construction, both transformations
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}
and
\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}
are covariant for
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant proper morphisms and they commute with exterior products.
The corresponding
equivariant Hirzebruch classes of
X
are defined as:

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X):={T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([id_{X}])\ ,\ \ \widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X):=\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([id_{X}]).

If
X
is smooth, then by (
87
) we get

(94)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=\sum_{p=0}^{\dim(X)}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([\Omega^{p}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}})\cdot y^{p}

The following result is an equivariant version of
[
36
]
[(1.1) and (2.13)], generalizing (
94
) to the singular toric context:

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
X
be a quasi-projective toric variety with torus
{\mathbb{T}}
. Then

(95)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)=\sum_{p=0}^{\dim(X)}[\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot y^{p}\in K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y],

where
\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
is the sheaf of Zariski
p
-forms on
X
. Moreover,

(96)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=\sum_{p=0}^{\dim(X)}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}})\cdot y^{p}.

In particular, the top degree in
y
of
mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)
, resp.,
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)
, is given by the equivariant class
[{\omega}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}
of the dualizing sheaf, resp.,
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\omega}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}})
. Similarly, for
y=0
, we get
mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)=[{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}
and
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{0*}(X)={\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
.

Proof.

First note that (
96
) is obtained by applying the transformation
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}
to (
95
).

Choose an equivariant cubic hyper-resolution
f_{\centerdot}:X_{\centerdot}\to X
of
X
(obtained by adapting to the equivariant context the results from
[
32
]
[Exposé V, Sect. 4]).
The natural map
\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}{\longrightarrow}Rf_{\centerdot*}\Omega^{p}_{X_{\centerdot}}
of
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant sheaf complexes is, by
[
3
]
[Thm.4.3], a quasi-isomorphism after forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action. In particular,
Rf_{\centerdot*}\Omega^{p}_{X_{\centerdot}}
is concentrated in degree zero, and also given by a reflexive sheaf (since this is the case for
\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
, see
[
16
]
[Prop.8.0.1, eqn.(8.0.5)]).
This map
then has to be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant isomorphism since the restrictions of these two
sheaves to the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant smooth locus
X_{sm}
of
X
are canonically isomorphic to
\Omega^{p}_{X_{sm}}
as
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant sheaves.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

The statement of Proposition
3.5
holds more generally, for

X
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed subvariety (i.e., a closed union of torus orbits, corresponding to a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
) in a quasi-projective toric variety with torus
{\mathbb{T}}
and fan
\Sigma
.
Instead of Zariski differential forms, one has to use the corresponding sheaves of Ishida differentials
\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
from
[
33
]
, with canonical sheaf
\omega_{X}:=\widetilde{\Omega}^{\dim(X)}_{X}
. To endow each
\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
with a canonical
{\mathbb{T}}
-action, one proceeds as indicated in
[
3
]
[Introduction], by realizing them as subsheaves of
f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{M}
with
f:M\to X
any
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant resolution of singularities. Note that these pushforward sheaves are independent of the choice of resolution by the birational invariance of
\Omega^{p}_{M}
.
In the notations used in the proof of Proposition
3.5
, the natural map
\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}{\longrightarrow}Rf_{\centerdot*}\Omega^{p}_{X_{\centerdot}}
of sheaf complexes is a quasi-isomorphism. If the
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant cubic hyper-resolution starts with a resolution of singularities
\epsilon_{0}:X_{0}\to X
, then this morphism is even
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant. This implies that

(97)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)=\sum_{p=0}^{\dim(X)}[\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}}\cdot y^{p}\in K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)[y].

Note also that
\widetilde{\Omega}^{0}_{X}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, as
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant sheaves.
If
X
is already a toric variety, these Ishida sheaves coincide with the Zariski sheaves, with the canonical
{\mathbb{T}}
-action, as used above.

Let
X_{\Sigma}
be a quasi-projective toric variety defined by a fan
\Sigma
, with
X:=X_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\subset X_{\Sigma}
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed algebraic subset of
X_{\Sigma}
defined by a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
. Let
O_{\sigma}
be the orbit of the cone
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\prime}
, with
i_{\sigma}:V_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow X
the orbit closure inclusion. Then, by additivity, the following equivariant version of
[
36
]
[Prop.3.1] holds:

Proposition 3.7
.

(98)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{\prime}}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow X])=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{\prime}}(i_{\sigma})_{*}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}]).

A similar formula holds for the equivariant Hirzebruch classes
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)
and
\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)
.

Moreover, toric geometry can be used as in
[
36
]
[Prop.3.2] to get the following (see also
[
44
]
[Cor.11.2]):

Proposition 3.8
.

For any cone
\sigma\in\Sigma
, one has:

(99)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}])=(1+y)^{\dim(\sigma)}\cdot[\omega_{V_{\sigma}}]_{\mathbb{T}},

with
\omega_{V_{\sigma}}
the canonical sheaf of
V_{\sigma}
, viewed as a
{\mathbb{T}}=T_{N}
-equivariant sheaf via the quotient map
T_{N}\to T_{N(\sigma)}
given by (
29
). Therefore,

(100)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}])=(1+y)^{\dim(\sigma)}\cdot{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([\omega_{V_{\sigma}}]_{\mathbb{T}}).

In particular, if
V_{\sigma}
is projective, then

(101)

\chi_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}]):={T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}\to pt])=(-1-y)^{\dim(O_{\sigma})}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[y]\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}[y].

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}\overset{f_{\sigma}}{\to}V_{\sigma}
be a toric resolution of singularities of
V_{\sigma}
. Then
\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}
is a toric variety obtained by refining the fan of
V_{\sigma}
. Let
O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}
be the natural open inclusion, with complement the simple normal crossing divisor
D_{\sigma}
whose irreducible components correspond to the rays in the fan of
\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}
. Note that
D_{\sigma}
is a
T_{N(\sigma)}
-invariant (hence a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant) divisor. Then formula (
91
) applied to the open inclusion
O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}
yields by the functoriality of
mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}
with respect to the proper morphism
f_{\sigma}
that:

(102)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}\big([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}]\big)=(f_{\sigma})_{*}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}(-D_{\sigma})\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}^{1}(\log D_{\sigma})]_{\mathbb{T}}\big).

However, since
\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}
is a smooth toric variety, the locally free sheaf
\Omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}^{1}(\log D_{\sigma})
is in fact
T_{N(\sigma)}
-equivariantly, hence also
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariantly, a trivial sheaf of rank equal to
\dim(\widetilde{V}_{\sigma})=\dim(O_{\sigma})
, e.g., see
[
16
]
[(8.1.5)].
So we get that:

(103)

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}(-D_{\sigma})\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}^{1}(\log D_{\sigma})]_{\mathbb{T}}=(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\sigma})}\cdot[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}(-D_{\sigma})]_{\mathbb{T}}\in K^{{\mathbb{T}}}_{0}(\widetilde{V}_{\sigma})[y].

Furthermore, note that the canonical dualizing sheaf
\omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}
on the toric variety
\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}
is precisely given by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}(-D_{\sigma})
, e.g., see
[
16
]
[Thm.8.2.3]. And since the toric morphism
f_{\sigma}:\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}\to V_{\sigma}
is induced by a refinement of the fan of
V_{\sigma}
, it follows from
[
16
]
[Thm.8.2.15] that there is a
T_{N(\sigma)}
-equivariant, hence also
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant, isomorphism:

(104)

{f_{\sigma}}_{*}\omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}\simeq\omega_{V_{\sigma}}.

Altogether, we obtain the following sequence of equalities:

(105)

\begin{split}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}\big([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma}]\big)&=(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\sigma})}\cdot(f_{\sigma})_{*}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}(-D_{\sigma})]_{\mathbb{T}}\big)\\
&=(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\sigma})}\cdot(f_{\sigma})_{*}\big([\omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}]_{\mathbb{T}}\big)\\
&=(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\sigma})}\cdot[\omega_{V_{\sigma}}]_{\mathbb{T}},\end{split}

where the last equality is a consequence of (
104
) and the vanishing of the higher derived image sheaves of
\omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}
, i.e.,

R^{i}(f_{\sigma})_{*}\omega_{\widetilde{V}_{\sigma}}=0
, for all
i>0
,
see
[
16
]
[Thm.9.3.12].

By applying the Todd class transformation
{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}
to equation (
99
), we obtain formula (
100
).

Finally, formula (
101
) follows from

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(V_{\sigma},\omega_{V_{\sigma}})=(-1)^{\dim(O_{\sigma})},

which is obtained by equivariant Serre duality and
\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(V_{\sigma},{\mathcal{O}}_{V_{\sigma}})=1
. The latter equality is just a special case of (
79
) for
D
the zero divisor, so that
{\mathcal{O}}(D)={\mathcal{O}}
.
∎

Using the functoriality of the equivariant motivic Chern and Hirzebruch classes, we get a relative version of Proposition
3.7
as follows. Let
f:X\to X^{\prime}
be a proper toric morphism of quasi-projective toric varieties, with the corresponding lattice homomorphism
f_{N}:N\to N^{\prime}
surjective (i.e.,
f
is a
toric fibration
in the sense of
[
21
]
[Prop.2.1]). Let
f_{{\mathbb{T}}}:{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
be the corresponding map of the associated tori, so that
{\mathbb{T}}
acts on
X^{\prime}
via
f
.
Let
\Sigma
,
\Sigma^{\prime}
be the fans of
X
, resp.,
X^{\prime}
. Since
f
is a toric fibration, a
{\mathbb{T}}
-orbit
O_{\sigma}
(
\sigma\in\Sigma
) is mapped by
f
to a
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
-orbit
f(O_{\sigma})=O_{\sigma^{\prime}}
(
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}
), such that the restriction map
f_{\sigma}=f|_{O_{\sigma}}:O_{\sigma}\to O_{\sigma^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to a projection
O_{\sigma}\simeq O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\times O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}\to O_{\sigma^{\prime}}
, with
O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}\simeq({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{\ell}
and
\ell=\dim(O_{\sigma})-\dim(O_{\sigma^{\prime}})
the relative dimension of
f_{\sigma}
(see
[
21
]
[Lem.2.6 and Prop.2.7]). Let
U\subset X
be a locally closed
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant subset (i.e., a locally closed union of
{\mathbb{T}}
-orbits of
X
), with

(106)

d_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime}):=|\Sigma_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime})|

and

(107)

\Sigma_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime}):=\{\sigma\in\Sigma\mid O_{\sigma}\subset U,\ f(O_{\sigma})=O_{\sigma^{\prime}},\ \ell=\dim(O_{\sigma})-\dim(O_{\sigma^{\prime}})\}.

Proposition 3.9
.

Under the above notations and assumptions, we have

(108)

\begin{split}f_{*}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([U\hookrightarrow X])&=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}d_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime})\cdot(-y-1)^{\ell}\cdot mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}])\\
&=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime})\cdot(1+y)^{\ell+\dim(O_{\sigma}^{\prime})}\cdot[\omega_{V_{\sigma^{\prime}}}]_{\mathbb{T}}.\end{split}

A similar formula holds for
f_{*}T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])
.

Proof.

By functoriality and additivity of
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
, we have

f_{*}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([U\hookrightarrow X])=mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([U\hookrightarrow X\overset{f}{\to}X^{\prime}])=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime})}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow X\overset{f}{\to}X^{\prime}]).

For
\ell\geq 0
,
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime})
fixed, we have

[O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow X\overset{f}{\to}X^{\prime}]=[O_{\sigma}\overset{f_{\sigma}}{\to}O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}].

Let us choose a splitting of the surjection
f_{\mathbb{T}}:{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
so that
{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\oplus{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
[
21
]
[Lem.2.6] and the proof of
[
21
]
[Prop.2.7], there is a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant isomorphism

O_{\sigma}\simeq O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\times O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}\to O_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,
with
{\mathbb{T}}
acting on
O_{\sigma^{\prime}}
by
f_{\mathbb{T}}:{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
and on
O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}
via the projection
{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}\to O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}
. Here, the surjective group homomorphism
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}\to O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}
is given in the proof of
[
21
]
[Prop.2.7].
We then have

[O_{\sigma}\overset{f_{\sigma}}{\to}O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}]=[O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}]\times[O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}\to pt].

Using the multiplicativity of
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}
, we get

mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\sigma}\overset{f_{\sigma}}{\to}O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}])=mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}])\boxtimes mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}\to pt]).

Here,
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}])
is calculated by (
99
) using the factorization
O_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow V_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
. Finally,
mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}\to pt])=(-1-y)^{\ell}
by (
101
) and using a projective toric compactification of
O_{\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}
.

The corresponding formula for the equivariant Hirzebruch class follows by applying the equivariant Todd transformation to (
108
).
∎

Remark 3.10
.

By forgetting the action, the corresponding non-equivariant version of (
108
) holds with the same proof, and even without the quasi-projectivity assumption.

Back to the general context, a choice of a splitting of a surjection of tori
{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
(as in the above results), yields an identification of equivariant characteristic classes with respect to
{\mathbb{T}}
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, as we explain next.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let the complex torus
{\mathbb{T}}
act on a quasi-projective variety
X
with a subtorus
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
acting trivially. Let
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}:={\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
and choose a compatible splitting of these tori and of their corresponding character lattices

{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\oplus{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}\ \ \text{\rm and}\ \ M=M^{\prime}\oplus M^{\prime\prime}.

Regard
X=X\times pt
, with the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action on the left corresponding to the product action of
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
on
X
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
on
pt
on the right.

These splittings then induce the following factorizations:

(a)

(equivariant K-theory)

(109)

\displaystyle K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq K^{0}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}K^{0}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}}(pt)

(110)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}[M]=K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt)

\displaystyle\simeq K^{0}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(pt)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}K^{0}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}}(pt)={\mathbb{Z}}[M^{\prime}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[M^{\prime\prime}]

(111)

\displaystyle K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq K_{0}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}K_{0}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}}(pt),

with (
109
) and (
110
) compatible under pullback, and (
109
) and (
111
) compatible under the module structure induced from the tensor product.

(b)

(equivariant (co)homology)

(112)

\displaystyle H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

(113)

\displaystyle Sym_{\mathbb{Q}}(M)=(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\simeq(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}}=Sym_{\mathbb{Q}}(M^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}Sym_{\mathbb{Q}}(M^{\prime\prime})

(114)

\displaystyle H_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle\simeq H_{*}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}},

with (
112
) and (
113
) compatible under pullback, and (
112
) and (
114
) compatible under the module structure induced from the cap product. Similar statements hold for the corresponding completions, as well as for equivariant Chow groups and their completions.

The above factorizations are compatible with the corresponding equivariant Chern character and Todd class transformations.

Tensoring with the distinguished elements
{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{0}}\in K^{0}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}}(pt)
and
1\in(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
induces inclusions of the
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
-equivariant groups on
X
(or pt) into the corresponding
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant groups on
X
(or pt). Moreover, these inclusions are independent of the choice of the compatible splittings of
{\mathbb{T}}
and
M
, respectively.

Proof.

The factorizations of (a) and (b) of (i) follow from suitable Künneth formulae.

Statement (ii) follows by construction from the multiplicativity under cross-products of the equivariant Chern character and equivariant Todd class transformation, see
[
23
]
.

It remains to justify the independence statement of part (iii). Two choices of splittings of
{\mathbb{T}}
differ by a group homomorphism
h:{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
acting on product classes
[-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime\prime}}}
of type (a) with
m^{\prime\prime}\in M^{\prime\prime}
via (compare
[
35
]
[Prop.2.14]):

[-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime\prime}}}\mapsto\left([-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\cdot{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime\prime}\circ h}}\right)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime\prime}}}.

So for
m^{\prime\prime}=0
, such an
h
acts trivially. Similarly, by choosing a basis
m_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,m_{r}^{\prime\prime}
of
M^{\prime\prime}
, one gets a basis
\prod_{i=1}^{r}c(m^{\prime\prime}_{i})^{k_{i}}
,
k_{i}\geq 0
, of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-vector space
(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Then

h
acts on product classes
[-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\otimes\prod_{i=1}^{r}c(m^{\prime\prime}_{i})^{k_{i}}
of type (b) via

[-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\otimes\prod_{i=1}^{r}c(m^{\prime\prime}_{i})^{k_{i}}\mapsto\left([-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}c(m^{\prime\prime}_{i}\circ h)^{k_{i}}\right)\otimes\prod_{i=1}^{r}c(m^{\prime\prime}_{i})^{k_{i}}.

So on product classes of type
[-]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\otimes 1
(i.e., with all
k_{i}
’s being
0
) it acts trivially.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

Results of Proposition
3.11
dealing only with rational equivariant (co)homology hold with the same proof under the weaker assumption of a splitting of rational vector spaces
M_{\mathbb{Q}}=M^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}}\oplus M^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Here
M_{\mathbb{Q}}:=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
, and similarly for
M^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
M^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

As a consequence, we have the following.

Corollary 3.13
.

In the setup of Proposition
3.11
, let
{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{F}}
be a
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundle, resp., coherent sheaf on
X
, with induced
{\mathbb{T}}
-action via the projection
{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
. Then

(115)

{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})={\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}([{\mathcal{E}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})\otimes 1\ \ \text{\rm and}\ \ \ {\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}]_{\mathbb{T}})={\rm td}_{*}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}([{\mathcal{F}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})\otimes 1.

Proof.

Choose a splitting
{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\oplus{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}
. Then, by part (ii) of Proposition
3.11
, we get:

\begin{split}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}]_{\mathbb{T}})&={\rm td}_{*}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}([{\mathcal{F}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})\otimes{\rm td}_{*}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}}({\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{0}})\\
&={\rm td}_{*}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}([{\mathcal{F}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})\otimes 1,\end{split}

and similarly for the equivariant Chern character.
∎

In particular, in the notations of Proposition
3.8
, a choice of splitting of the surjection of tori
{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}:=T_{N(\sigma)}
yields an identification

(116)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([\omega_{V_{\sigma}}]_{\mathbb{T}})={\rm td}_{*}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}([\omega_{V_{\sigma}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}),

which is independent of the splitting.

3.2.
Generalized equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch

Let
X_{\Sigma}
be a projective toric variety defined by a fan
\Sigma
, with
X:=X_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\subset X_{\Sigma}
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed algebraic subset of
X_{\Sigma}
defined by a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
.
Let
D
be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor on
X_{\Sigma}
. The
equivariant Hirzebruch polynomial of
D|_{X}
is defined by the formula:

(117)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\Sigma}}(D)|_{X}):=\sum_{p=0}^{\dim(X)}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\Sigma}}(D)|_{X})\cdot y^{p}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}[y]\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}[y]\>,

with
\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
denoting as in Remark
3.6

the sheaf of Ishida
p
-forms on
X
.

Then we have the following result:

Theorem 3.14
.

(Generalized equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch)

In the above setup, the equivariant Hirzebruch polynomial of
D|_{X}
is computed by the formula:

(118)

\begin{split}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\Sigma}}(D)|_{X})&=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\Sigma}}(D)|_{X})\cap T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X).\end{split}

Proof.

This follows as in
[
36
]
[Theorem 2.4], by making use of Proposition
3.5
, Remark
3.6
and the module property (
81
) of
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}
.
∎

As a consequence, we obtain the following weighted version of formula (
75
):

Corollary 3.15
.

Let
P
be a full-dimensional lattice polytope with associated projective toric variety
X_{P}
and ample Cartier divisor
D=D_{P}
. Let
X:=X_{P^{\prime}}
be the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed algebraic subset of
X_{P}
corresponding to a polytopal subcomplex
P^{\prime}\subset P
(i.e., a closed union of faces of
P
). Then:

(119)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})=\sum_{E\preceq P^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(E)}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M}e^{s(m)}.

Proof.

For a face
E
of
P^{\prime}
, denote by
i_{E}:V_{\sigma_{E}}:=X_{E}\hookrightarrow X
the inclusion of the orbit closure associated to the (cone of the) face
E
.
Note that we have
\dim(E)=\dim(O_{\sigma_{E}}).
Let
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}:=T_{N(\sigma_{E})}
be the quotient torus of
{\mathbb{T}}
corresponding to
X_{E}
.

Then, by Theorem
3.14
, Proposition
3.7
and Proposition
3.8
, the following equality holds:

\begin{split}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})&=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})\cap T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)\\
&=\sum_{E\preceq P^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(E)}\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})\cap(i_{E})_{*}{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}}).\end{split}

It remains to prove that for any face
E
of
P^{\prime}
, we have that:

(120)

\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})\cap(i_{E})_{*}{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})=\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M}e^{s(m)}.

This follows from the functorial properties of the cap product and formula (
43
). Indeed,

\begin{split}\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})\cap(i_{E})_{*}{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})&=\int_{X_{E}}(i_{E})^{*}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})\\
&=\int_{X_{E}}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}((i_{E})^{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X}))\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})\\
&\overset{(\ref{tran})}{=}\int_{X_{E}}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{E}}(D_{E_{0}}-div(\chi^{m_{0}})))\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}])\\
&=e^{-c(m_{0})}\int_{X_{E}}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{E}}(D_{E_{0}}))\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})\\
&\overset{({*})}{=}e^{-c(m_{0})}\int_{X_{E}}{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{E}}(D_{E_{0}}))\cap{\rm td}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{*}([\omega_{X_{E}}]_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})\\
&\overset{(\ref{lpc2})}{=}e^{s(m_{0})}\cdot\left(\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E-m_{0})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\\
&=\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M}e^{s(m)},\end{split}

where (*) uses Proposition
3.11
and Corollary
3.13
. Here
m_{0}
is a vertex of
E
as in (
43
), so that
E_{0}:=E-m_{0}
is a full-dimensional lattice polytope in
Span(E_{0})
relative to the lattice
Span(E_{0})\cap M
, with
X_{E}
the associated toric variety.
∎

Remark 3.16
.

For future use, we include here the following formula, which one gets as in the proof of (
120
), but using
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{E}}
instead of
\omega_{X_{E}}
:

(121)

\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X_{P}}(D)|_{X})\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}((i_{E})_{*}[{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}})=\sum_{m\in E\cap M}e^{s(m)}.

This is also a special case of Corollary
3.15
for
y=0
and
P^{\prime}=E
.

As another application, we employ Theorem
3.14
in the context of a
globally generated

{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor
D
on a projective toric variety
X
with associated torus
{\mathbb{T}}
. Let
P_{D}\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
be the lattice polytope corresponding to
D
, and let
X_{D}
be the toric variety of the lattice polytope
P_{D}
, defined via the corresponding
generalized fan
as in
[
16
]
[Prop.6.2.3]. By
[
16
]
[Thm.6.2.8], there is a proper toric morphism
f:X\to X_{D}
, induced by the corresponding lattice projection
N\to N_{D}
given by dividing out by the minimal cone of the generalized fan of
P_{D}
. In particular,
f:X\to X_{D}
is a toric fibration. Let
M_{D}\hookrightarrow M
be the associated inclusion of dual lattices. Choosing a vertex
m_{0}
of
P_{D}
, we get that
P^{\prime}:=P_{D}-m_{0}\subset M_{D}
is a full-dimensional lattice polytope. Let
D^{\prime}
be the ample divisor on
X_{D}
associated to
P^{\prime}
, with
\Sigma^{\prime}
the inner normal fan of
P^{\prime}
(defining
X_{D}
), so that there is a one-to-one correspondence between cones
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}
and faces
E^{\prime}
of the lattice polytope
P^{\prime}
, and by translation to the faces
E
of
P_{D}
. Then, by the proof of
[
16
]
[Thm.6.2.8], one gets that
{\mathcal{O}}(D-div(\chi^{m_{0}}))\simeq f^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(D^{\prime})
, as
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant sheaves.

Following the notations of Proposition
3.9
, let
U\subset X
be a closed
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant subset with associated multiplicities
d_{\ell}(U/\sigma^{\prime})
, denoted here via the above correspondence as
d_{\ell}(U/E)
. We can now prove the following.

Corollary 3.17
.

With the above notations,

(122)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U})=\sum_{E\preceq P_{D}}\left(\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/E)\cdot(1+y)^{\ell+\dim(E)}\right)\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M}e^{s(m)}.

Proof.

By the generalized Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch Theorem
3.14
, the projection formula, and functoriality of Hirzebruch classes, we have

\begin{split}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U})&=\int_{U}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U})\cap T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(U)\\
&=\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))\cap T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])\\
&=e^{s(m_{0})}\int_{X}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}(f^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D^{\prime}))\cap T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])\\
&=e^{s(m_{0})}\int_{X_{D}}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D^{\prime}))\cap f_{*}T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])\\
&\overset{(\ast)}{=}e^{s(m_{0})}\left(\sum_{E^{\prime}\preceq P^{\prime}}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/E^{\prime})\cdot(1+y)^{\ell+\dim(E^{\prime})}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E^{\prime})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\\
&=\sum_{E\preceq P_{D}}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/E)\cdot(1+y)^{\ell+\dim(E)}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M}e^{s(m)},\end{split}

where the equality
({\ast})
follows by using (
108
) and (
120
).
∎

Example 3.18

(Rigidity of the equivariant
\chi_{y}
-genus)
.

If, in Corollary
3.17
, we take
D=0
, then
P_{D}=\{0\}\subset M\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
, so

d_{\ell}(U/\{0\})
is just the number of
\ell
-dimensional torus orbits in
U
. Hence, formula
(
122
) becomes in this case

(123)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{U})=\left(\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/\{0\})\cdot(1+y)^{\ell}\right)\cdot e^{s(0)}.

Note that this formula does not take into account any equivariant information. So, forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action,

\left(\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/\{0\})\cdot(1+y)^{\ell}\right)=\chi_{y}(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{U}).

In particular, for
U=X
and
y=0
, this becomes
\chi(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=1
. ∎

Remark 3.19
.

Note that if
D
is an ample
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor, then the above morphism
f
is the identity of
X
, so formula (
122
) reduces to
(
119
) since the multiplicities
d_{\ell}(U/E)
are given as follows:
d_{\ell}(U/E)=0
for
\ell>0
, and
d_{0}(U/E)
is either
1
or
0
, depending wether the orbit associated to
E
is included or not in
U
.

Remark 3.20
.

By forgetting the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action in formula (
122
), we get the following weighted lattice point counting for lattice polytopes associated to globally generated
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisors. More precisely, in the above notations, we get the following generalization of Theorem
2.3
and of
[
36
]
[Cor.1.8].

(124)

\chi_{y}(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U})=\sum_{E\preceq P_{D}}\left(\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(-1)^{\ell}\cdot d_{\ell}(U/E)\cdot(1+y)^{\ell+\dim(E)}\right)\cdot|{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M|.

Here, it is enough to assume that
X
is complete, since the non-equivariant Hirzebruch class transformation is defined in this generality.

3.3.
Equivariant Hirzebruch and Todd characteristic classes of simplicial toric varieties

The previously mentioned formulae of Subsection
2.4
for the Todd class and Hirzebruch classes of
quasi-projective simplicial toric varieties hold with the same proofs (up to some small modifications, as explained below) for the equivariant versions of these characteristic classes, compare also with
[
24
]
[Rem.4.3]. A different proof of all these results, independent of the equivariant version of the Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch from
[
24
]
[Thm.3.1 and Rem.4.3], will be given in Subsection
4.3
via localization at the torus fixed points. Nevertheless, the approach of this subsection is useful to derive global expressions for the equivariant characteristic classes of interest, which will then be localized.

Let
X:=X_{\Sigma}
be a simplicial quasi-projective toric variety associated to a fan
\Sigma
of full-dimensional convex support, e.g.,
X=X_{P}
is the semi-projective simplicial toric variety corresponding to a simple full-dimensional lattice polyhedron
P\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
. This includes the cases of full-dimensional simple lattice polytopes, as well as full-dimensional rational pointed polyhedral cones. The assumption that
\Sigma
is of full-dimensional convex support implies that
X
contains no torus factors, so we can use the Cox construction as described in Subsection
2.1.2
. Moreover, this allows us to make use of Remark
2.8
.

In addition, we also prove formulae for the equivariant Hirzebruch classes
T_{y*}^{\mathbb{T}}([U\hookrightarrow X])
, with
U
the open complement of a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor
D_{K}:=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}D_{\rho}
, for
K\subset\Sigma(1)
. Similarly, we
indicate here the argument even for
the Hirzebruch classes of an orbit closure
V_{\tau}
, for
\tau\in\Sigma
, pointing out the needed modifications in the proof.

The arguments here are based on the Cox construction. With the notations from Subsection
2.1.2
, let

\pi:W:={\mathbb{C}}^{r}\setminus Z(\Sigma)\to X
be the toric morphism, with
G
the kernel of the corresponding map of tori
\gamma:\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}:=({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r}\to{\mathbb{T}}
(here
r=|\Sigma(1)|
). Since
X
is a simplicial toric variety containing no torus factor,
it follows that
X
is the geometric quotient
W/G
, with
G
acting with finite stabilizers.
If
\tau
is a cone of
\Sigma
, then the orbit closure
V_{\tau}
is the image under
\pi
of a linear subspace
W_{\tau}\subset W
. We have the following formula (in which we omit the symbols for pushforwards under closed embeddings).

Lemma 3.21
.

(125)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}((\pi_{*}\Lambda_{y}T^{*}_{W_{\tau}})^{G})=(1+y)^{r-n}\cdot{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}(V_{\tau}))\in\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})[y]\simeq\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})[y].

Proof.

By (
65
), it suffices to check formula (
125
) after restriction to each
U_{\sigma}
, with
U_{\sigma}
the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant open affine subset of
X
containing the corresponding
{\mathbb{T}}
-fixed point
x_{\sigma}
, for
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
. We have
\pi^{-1}(U_{\sigma})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
, with
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\simeq{\mathbb{T}}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}=:{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
acting on the respective factors, and the factor
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
corresponding to the rays of
\sigma
and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}=({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
acting freely by multiplication on itself. Similarly,
G\simeq G_{\sigma}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
, with
G_{\sigma}\subset{\mathbb{T}}
a finite subgroup. So, above
U_{\sigma}
,
\pi
can be factorized as a composition of the free quotient
\widetilde{\pi}:{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
by the
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
-action, followed by a finite quotient map
\pi=\pi_{\sigma}:{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n}/{G_{\sigma}}=U_{\sigma}
.
Similarly,
\pi^{-1}(U_{\sigma}\cap V_{\tau})\simeq L_{\tau}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
, with
L_{\tau}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
a linear subspace. Then

\big(\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\Lambda_{y}T^{*}_{L_{\tau}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}}\big)^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}=\Lambda_{y}T^{*}_{L_{\tau}}\cdot(1+y)^{r-n},

since
T^{*}_{({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}}\simeq({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}\times T^{*}_{id}
, with
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
acting by the co-adjoint action on the cotangent space
T^{*}_{id}
of
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
at the identity element
id\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
. This is the trivial
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
-bundle of rank
r-n
, since
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
is an abelian group, so that

\Lambda_{y}T^{*}_{({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}}=(1+y)^{r-n}
.
Finally, in
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma})[y]
, we have by Proposition
3.5
and Corollary
3.13
that

((\pi_{\sigma})_{*}\Lambda_{y}T^{*}_{L_{\tau}})^{G_{\sigma}}=mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(V_{\tau}\cap U_{\sigma}).

The desired formula (
125
) follows by applying the equivariant Todd transformation.
∎

By the equivariant version of the Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch theorem (see
[
24
]
[Thm.3.1 and Rem.4.3]), applied to the left-hand side of formula (
125
), the proof of the formulae of Subsection
2.4
for the Todd class and Hirzebruch classes of
projective simplicial toric varieties hold with the same proofs in the equivariant setting.
For later applications, we work with the cohomological images
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
and
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})[y]
,
resp.,
\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}[y]):=\prod_{i\geq 0}H^{i}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}[y])

of these classes under equivariant Poincaré duality.
In the notations of Subsection
2.1.2
, one has the following:

Theorem 3.22
.

Let
X:=X_{\Sigma}
be a simplicial quasi-projective toric variety associated to a fan
\Sigma
of full-dimensional convex support.
Then the equivariant Hirzebruch classes
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)
and
\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)
are computed by:

(126)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=(1+y)^{n-r}\cdot\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})[y]\>,

(127)

\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}[y])\>,

with
r=|\Sigma(1)|
, and
F_{\rho}=[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}
denoting the equivariant fundamental class of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor
D_{\rho}
corresponding to the ray
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
.

Corollary 3.23
.

If
X:=X_{\Sigma}
is a smooth quasi-projective toric variety associated to a fan
\Sigma
of full-dimensional convex support, then
G_{\Sigma}
is the trivial group.
Hence, the equivariant Hirzebruch classes of
X
are given by:

(128)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=(1+y)^{n-r}\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-e^{-F_{\rho}}}

(129)

\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}\big)}{1-e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}}\>.

Remark 3.24
.

In particular, by setting
y=0
in Theorem
3.22
, we recover the equivariant Todd class formula of
[
13
]
given by the following expression in
\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
:

(130)

\begin{split}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)&=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}.\end{split}

Similarly, setting
y=0
in Corollary
3.23
, one gets the equivariant Todd class
for such a smooth quasi-projective toric variety.
There is also an equivariant version of formula (
56
). Finally, if
X
is projective, the specialization at
y=1
fits with suitable
L
-classes, i.e.,
\widehat{T}_{1*}(X)=L(X)
is the Thom-Milnor
L
-class of a projective toric variety
X
.

Moreover, taking the top degree of
y
in (
126
) and using (
96
), yields the following.

Corollary 3.25
.

Under the assumptions and notations of Theorem
3.22
, we have:

(131)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([\omega_{X}]_{\mathbb{T}})=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{a_{\rho}(g)\cdot F_{\rho}\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}).

Remark 3.26
.

Note that formula (
131
) coincides with (
130
) upon substituting
-F_{\rho}
for
F_{\rho}
, for each
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
, i.e., these classes are exchanged by the
cohomological duality involution
on
\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
given by multiplication with
(-1)^{i}
in degree
2i
. (Recall that under our assumptions
\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
is even, by (
63
).)

In Section
5
below, formula (
131
) will be used for proving Euler-Maclaurin type formulae for lattice points in the interior of a full-dimensional polytope, generalizing formula (
46
) for lattice point counting. The next results are motivated by the fact that, instead of deleting all facets of the polytope
P
, one can just delete some of the facets
F_{i}
,
i\in K\subset\Sigma_{P}(1)
.

Lemma 3.27
.

Let
X:=X_{\Sigma}
be a simplicial quasi-projective toric variety associated to a fan
\Sigma
of full-dimensional convex support.
Let
U\subset X
be the open complement of the divisor
D_{K}:=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}D_{\rho}
, for
K\subset\Sigma(1)
.
In the notations
of the Cox construction, let
W_{\rho}=\{x_{\rho}=0\}\subset W
be the inverse image of
D_{\rho}
under the quotient map
\pi:W\to X
. Then the preimage
\widetilde{U}
of
U
under
\pi
is the complement of the
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
-invariant normal crossing divisor
W_{K}=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}W_{\rho}
in
W
, and

(132)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}\big((\pi_{*}mC_{y}^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}([\widetilde{U}\hookrightarrow W]))^{G}\big)=(1+y)^{r-n}\cdot{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([U\hookrightarrow X]))\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})[y].

Proof.

By the inclusion-exclusion formula for the equivariant motivic Chern classes, one has as in (
92
),

(133)

mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([U\hookrightarrow X])=\sum_{I\subset K}(-1)^{|I|}mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([D_{I}\hookrightarrow X])\>,

with
D_{I}=\bigcap_{\rho\in I}D_{\rho}
. Similarly,

(134)

mC^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}_{y}([\widetilde{U}\hookrightarrow W])=\sum_{I\subset K}(-1)^{|I|}mC^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}_{y}([W_{I}\hookrightarrow W]),

with
W_{I}=\bigcap_{\rho\in I}W_{\rho}
.
The assertion follows now by applying formula (
125
) to the summands on the right-hand side of the two identities above.∎

By formula (
91
), in the above notations we have:

(135)

mC^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}_{y}([\widetilde{U}\hookrightarrow W])=[{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{K})\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{W}(\log W_{K})]_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}\in K_{0}^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(W)[y]\>.

Then by the equivariant version of the Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch theorem (see
[
24
]
[Thm.3.1 and Rem.4.3]), applied to the right-hand side of formula (
135
), we get the following generalization of Theorem
3.22
:

Theorem 3.28
.

Let
X:=X_{\Sigma}
be a simplicial quasi-projective toric variety associated to a fan
\Sigma
of full-dimensional convex support.
Let
U\subset X
be the open complement of the divisor
D_{K}:=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}D_{\rho}
, for
K\subset\Sigma(1)
.
The equivariant Hirzebruch classes
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])
and
\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])
are computed by:

(136)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])=(1+y)^{n-r}\cdot\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\prod_{\rho\notin K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\>,

(137)

\widehat{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([U\hookrightarrow X])=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}}\prod_{\rho\notin K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}(1+y)}}\>,

with
r=|\Sigma(1)|
, and
F_{\rho}=[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}
denoting the equivariant fundamental class of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor
D_{\rho}
corresponding to the ray
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
3.22
and is based on the equivariant Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch theorem. We only indicate here the changes. Instead of calculating the equivariant twisted Chern character
ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}-)(g)
,
g\in G
, of
\Lambda_{y}T^{*}_{W}
for
i_{g}:W^{g}\hookrightarrow W
the fixed point set inclusion, one now has to calculate this for
{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{K})\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{W}(\log W_{K})
.
Here, for
{\mathcal{E}}\in K^{0}_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(W)
, the twisted equivariant Chern character is defined as

ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}{\mathcal{E}})(g)=\sum_{\chi}\chi(g)\cdot ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}),

where
{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\oplus_{\chi}{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
is the finite decomposition of
{\mathcal{E}}
into sheaves
{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
on which
g
acts by a (complex-valued) character
\chi
. These sheaves
{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
are also
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
-equivariant since
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
is an abelian group.

We now have to evaluate
ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}-)(g)
on

{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{K})\otimes\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{W}(\log W_{K})\simeq\prod_{\rho\in K}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{\rho})\otimes pr_{\rho}^{*}\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}(\log\{0\})\big)\prod_{\rho\notin K}\Lambda_{y}{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{\rho}).

Here
pr_{\rho}:W\to{\mathbb{C}}
is the projection to the
\rho
-th factor, with
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\simeq({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r}
acting factorwise via the projection
a_{\rho}:\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
to the corresponding
\rho
-th factor. So the calculation can be done factorwise, with

ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}\Lambda_{y}{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{\rho}))(g)=i_{g}^{*}\left(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-z_{\rho}}\right),

for
\rho\notin K
, and
z_{\rho}:=[W_{\rho}]_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}\in H^{*}_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(W;{\mathbb{Q}})
the corresponding equivariant fundamental class as in
[
36
]
[eqn.(5.13)]. By the inclusion-exclusion formula, for
\{0\}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
and
\rho\in K
, we get:

[{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{\rho})\otimes pr_{\rho}^{*}\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}(\log\{0\})]=[\Lambda_{y}{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{\rho})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{\rho}}].

So

\begin{split}&ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}\left(i_{g}^{*}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-W_{\rho})\otimes pr_{\rho}^{*}\Lambda_{y}\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}(\log\{0\})\big)\right)(g)\\
&=i_{g}^{*}\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-z_{\rho}}\big)-ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}\left([{\mathcal{O}}_{W}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{W}(-D_{\rho})]\right))(g)\\
&=i_{g}^{*}\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-z_{\rho}}\big)-i_{g}^{*}\big(1-a_{\rho}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-z_{\rho}}\big)\\
&=i_{g}^{*}\left((1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-z_{\rho}}\right).\end{split}

Then these formulae are pushed forward via
(i_{g})_{*}
using the projection formula, and one applies the ring isomorphism
\phi:H^{*}_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(W;{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}_{{{\mathbb{T}}}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Note that
z_{\rho}
maps to
F_{\rho}
under the identification
\phi:H^{2}_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(W;{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{2}_{{{\mathbb{T}}}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Finally, note that
g\in G_{\Sigma}
if and only if
g^{-1}\in G_{\Sigma}
. This completes the proof for the un-normalized equivariant Hirzebruch classes.
To get the formula for the normalized equivariant Hirzebruch classes, we just have to substitute
(1+y)^{-n}\cdot[X]_{{\mathbb{T}}}
for
[X]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\in H^{\mathbb{T}}_{2n}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
(implicitly used in the equivariant Poincaré duality), and

(1+y)\cdot F_{\rho}
for
F_{\rho}\in H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
.
∎

For later use, and since the needed notation was already introduced in the previous result, let us sketch a proof of the following extension of the Todd class formula (
130
) to the
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf
\pi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{W}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{\widetilde{m}}})^{G}
, for
\pi:W\to X
the quotient map of the Cox construction and
\chi^{\widetilde{m}}
a character of
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}
.

Lemma 3.29
.

With the above notations, we have

(138)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(\pi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{W}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{\widetilde{m}}})^{G})=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\chi^{\widetilde{m}}(g^{-1})\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot e^{\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}},

with
\{e_{\rho}\}_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}
the standard basis of the lattice
\widetilde{N}={\mathbb{Z}}^{|\Sigma(1)|}
.

Proof.

Comparing to the proof of the classical Todd class formula (
130
), we need to calculate

ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{W}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{\widetilde{m}}}\big))(g)
instead of
ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{W}))(g)=1
. This is given by

ch^{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(i_{g}^{*}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{W}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{\widetilde{m}}}\big))(g)=i_{g}^{*}\left(\chi^{\widetilde{m}}(g)e^{c(\widetilde{m})}\right),

with
c(\widetilde{m})=\sum_{\rho}\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle z_{\rho}\in H^{2}_{\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}}(W;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
,
e.g., see
[
16
]
[Prop.12.4.13(b)].
∎

Remark 3.30
.

Note that if
K=\emptyset
, Theorem
3.28
reduces to Theorem
3.22
. At the other extreme, if
K=\Sigma(1)
, one gets for the un-normalized class first by specializing to
y=0
just Corollary
3.25
, and using this one then recovers for arbitrary
y
formula (
100
) in the case of the zero cone.

Remark 3.31
.

For
y=0
, both formulae of Theorem
3.28
specialize to the following Todd class type formula, which will fit later on with the Euler-Maclaurin formulas for polytopes with some facets removed:

(139)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{0*}([U\hookrightarrow X])=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\prod_{\rho\notin K}\frac{F_{\rho}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\>.

Note that in view of Remark
3.6
