, one also has

(140)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{0*}([U\hookrightarrow X])=td_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)-td_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(D_{K}).

4.
Localization in equivariant
K
-theory and applications

In this section we apply localization techniques in
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant
K
-theory (Subsection
4.1
) and
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant homology theory (Subsection
4.2
) of toric varieties, due to Brion-Vergne
[
13
]
and, resp., Brylinski-Zhang
[
15
]
, for the calculation of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant motivic Chern and Hirzebruch classes in this toric context.
In the simplicial context, these localizations can be made explicit by using a Lefschetz type Euler characteristic in the local affine context,
instead of using the global equivariant Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch theorem.
Finally, we recast in Subsection
4.3
the formulae of the previous section for the
equivariant Hirzebruch classes of simplicial toric varieties via localization at the torus fixed points.

4.1.
Localization in equivariant K-theory

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be an
n
-dimensional toric variety with torus
{\mathbb{T}}=T_{N}
such that the fixed-point set
X^{\mathbb{T}}\neq\emptyset
, e.g.,
X
is projective.

By
[
13
]
[Prop.1.5], the inclusion
i:X^{\mathbb{T}}\hookrightarrow X
induces an injective morphism of
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(pt)
-modules

i_{*}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}})\hookrightarrow K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)

which becomes an isomorphism

i_{*}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}})_{S}\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)_{S}

upon localization at the multiplicative subset
S\subset{\mathbb{Z}}[M]=K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(pt)
generated by the elements
1-\chi^{m}
, for
0\neq m\in M
. Note that

K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}})=\bigoplus_{x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}}K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(x),

from which one gets via the isomorphism
i_{*}
a projection map of
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(pt)
-modules, called here the
K
-theoretic localization map at
x
,

pr_{x}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)_{S}\simeq\bigoplus_{x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}}K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(x)_{S}\longrightarrow K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(x)_{S}={\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}.

Let
x=x_{\sigma}\subset U_{\sigma}
be the
{\mathbb{T}}
-fixed point corresponding to a cone
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
of maximal dimension, as in (
65
), where
U_{\sigma}\subset X
is the unique
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant open affine subset containing
x_{\sigma}=O_{\sigma}
. Then the localization map at
x
factorizes via restriction over
U_{\sigma}
as:

pr_{x}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)_{S}\longrightarrow K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma})_{S}\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma})_{S}={\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}.

We now explain a different description of the isomorphism (denoted also by
pr_{x}
)

pr_{x}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma})_{S}\simeq K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma})_{S}={\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}

by using the eigenspace decomposition (
68
). As in
[
13
]
[Sect.1.3] (see also
[
16
]
[Def.13.2.2]), a formal power series
f\in{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]
is called
summable
if there is
g\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M]
and a finite subset
I\subset M\setminus\{0\}
such that in
{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]
one has:
f\cdot\prod_{m\in I}(1-\chi^{m})=g
. Let

\mathbb{S}(f):=g\cdot\prod_{m\in I}(1-\chi^{m})^{-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}

be the
sum
of
f
, which is easily seen to be independent of the factorization.
Let
{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]_{\rm Sum}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]
be the subset of summable elements in
{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]
. This is a
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]
-submodule of
{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]
, and the summation map
\mathbb{S}
induces a homomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]
-modules

\mathbb{S}:{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]_{\rm Sum}\to{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf on
U_{\sigma}
. Then
W:=H^{0}(U_{\sigma};{\mathcal{F}})
has an eigenspace decomposition
W=\bigoplus_{m\in M}W_{\chi^{m}}
(see
[
13
]
[Sect.1.3]) with eigenspaces
W_{\chi^{m}}
of finite dimension as in (
68
) (although
W=H^{0}(U_{\sigma};{\mathcal{F}})
could be infinite dimensional). Then

(141)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{F}}):=\sum_{m\in M}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}W_{\chi^{m}}\cdot\chi^{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]

is summable (cf.
[
13
]
[Prop.1.3]). This induces a homomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]
-modules (see
[
13
]
[Rem.1.3])

(142)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma})\longrightarrow\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}

such that the composition
\mathbb{S}\circ\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}
induces after localization the map
pr_{x}
, since

(143)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(i_{*}([{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m}}])=\chi^{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\subset\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}.

For applications to the case of simplicial cones, we next introduce a Lefschetz type variant of the Euler characteristic
\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}
, and a corresponding summation map
\mathbb{S}
. Let
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
be a simplicial cone with
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\in N=N_{\sigma}
the generators of the the rays
\rho_{j}\in\sigma(1)
,
j=1,\ldots,n
. Let
N^{\prime}=N^{\prime}_{\sigma}
be the finite index sublattice of
N
generated by
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}
, and consider
\sigma\in N^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{R}}}=N_{\mathbb{R}}
so that it is smooth with respect to the lattice
N^{\prime}
. With
{\mathbb{T}}
,
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
the corresponding
n
-dimensional tori of the lattices
N
, resp.,
N^{\prime}
, the inclusion
N^{\prime}\hookrightarrow N
induces a toric morphism
\pi:U^{\prime}_{\sigma}\to U_{\sigma}
of the associated affine toric varieties. Let
G_{\sigma}
be the finite kernel of the epimorphism
\pi:{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{T}}
, so that
U^{\prime}_{\sigma}/G_{\sigma}\simeq U_{\sigma}
(e.g., see
[
16
]
[Prop.1.3.18]).

Let
m_{i}\in M=M_{\sigma}
,
1\leq i\leq n
, be the unique primitive elements in the dual lattice
M
of
N
satisfying
\langle m_{i},u_{j}\rangle=0
for
i\neq j
and

q_{i}:=\langle m_{i},u_{i}\rangle>0

so that the dual lattice
M^{\prime}=M_{\sigma}^{\prime}
of
N^{\prime}
is generated by the elements
m^{\prime}_{j}:=\frac{m_{j}}{q_{j}}.

Let
a_{\rho_{j}}:G_{\sigma}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
be the characters of
G_{\sigma}
as introduced in (
33
).

For
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
a
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
-equivariant coherent sheaf on
U^{\prime}_{\sigma}
, the vector space
W^{\prime}:=H^{0}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma};{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
has an eigenspace decomposition
W^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}}W^{\prime}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}}
as before. Since
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
is abelian, its finite subgroup
G_{\sigma}
acts on
W^{\prime}
respecting this eigenspace decomposition. We can then introduce the Lefschetz type Euler characteristic

(144)

tr^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\bigoplus_{m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}tr(g:W^{\prime}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}}\to W^{\prime}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}})\cdot\chi^{m^{\prime}}\in{\mathbb{C}}[[M^{\prime}]]_{\rm sum}.

In this context, the notion of
summable
is defined almost as above, but using the multiplicative subset
S^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]
generated by elements
1-a\cdot\chi^{m^{\prime}}
, for
0\neq m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
and
a\in{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. This induces a homomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}[M^{\prime}]
-modules

tr^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}:K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma})\longrightarrow\mbox{$\mathbb{C}$}[M^{\prime}]_{\rm sum}.

The fact that
tr^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
is summable will be explained below for sheaves
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}}
, with
m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
, whose classes generate
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma})
(as in the proof of
[
13
]
[Cor.1.2]). There is also a corresponding summation map

\mathbb{S}^{\prime}:{\mathbb{C}}[[M^{\prime}]]_{\rm sum}\to{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}},

so that the following diagram of
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]
-linear maps commutes:

(145)

\begin{CD}K_{0}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma})@>{tr^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}}>{}>{\mathbb{C}}[[M^{\prime}]]_{\rm sum}@>{\mathbb{S}^{\prime}}>{}>{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}\\
@V{\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}}V{}V@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma})@>{\chi^{{\mathbb{T}}}_{\sigma}}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]_{\rm sum}@>{\mathbb{S}}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}\ .\end{CD}

Here,
\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}
is induced by the corresponding exact functor given by taking the
G_{\sigma}
-invariant part of the pushforward for the finite map
\pi
, which is
{\mathbb{T}}={\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}/G_{\sigma}
-equivariant. The monomorphism
{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]_{\rm sum}\to{\mathbb{C}}[[M^{\prime}]]_{\rm sum}
and the algebra map
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}\to{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}
are induced by the lattice injection
M\hookrightarrow M^{\prime}
.

Example 4.1
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}}
, then

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{m\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}.

If, moreover,
\sigma
is a smooth cone with
m_{\sigma,i}
,
i=1,\ldots,n
, the minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
, then

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{k\geq 0}(\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}})^{k}),

hence (as in
[
16
]
[Lem.13.2.4])

(146)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}}))=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}.

If
\sigma
is only a simplicial cone, with the same notation for the minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
, we get for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}
that

tr_{\sigma}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}})=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{k\geq 0}(a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}})^{k}),

hence this series is summable, and
by applying diagram (
145
) to
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}
, with
{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\sigma}}\simeq\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}({\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}})
, we recover
Molien’s formula
(see
[
13
]
[page 24]):

(147)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}}))=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}\in{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}.

Recall here that, by our convention (following
[
16
]
),
{\mathbb{T}}
acts on
\chi^{m}
by
t\cdot\chi^{m}=\chi^{m}(t^{-1})\chi^{m}
. Note that
[
13
]
uses Oda’s convention
[
39
]
[pag.6]:
t\cdot\chi^{m}=\chi^{m}(t)\chi^{m}
, which explains the sign difference. ∎

Example 4.2
.

Consider the case of a simplicial cone
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
with minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
as before. We get for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}}
(with
m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
) that

tr_{\sigma}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}})=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\chi^{m^{\prime}}(g)\cdot\chi^{m^{\prime}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{k\geq 0}(a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}})^{k}),

hence this series is summable, and
by applying diagram (
145
) to
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}}
, with
{\mathcal{F}}:=\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}({\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}})
, we get

(148)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{F}}))=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\chi^{m^{\prime}}(g)\cdot\chi^{m^{\prime}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}\in{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}.

∎

Example 4.3
.

Back to the general case, for
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
, consider
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U_{\sigma}}
, with
D
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor on
X
. Then one gets

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U_{\sigma}})=\chi^{-m_{\sigma}}\sum_{m\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum},

where
m_{\sigma}\in M
is uniquely defined so that
D|_{U_{\sigma}}=div(\chi^{-m_{\sigma}})|_{U_{\sigma}}
. The sequence
\{m_{\sigma}\in M\mid\sigma\in\Sigma(n)\}
is the Cartier data of
D
, in the sense of
[
16
]
[Thm.4.2.8]. In other words,
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)|_{U_{\sigma}}\simeq U_{\sigma}\times{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{-m_{\sigma}}}
as
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant line bundles (e.g., see
[
16
]
[page 609]), so that for any
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf on
X
, we have

(149)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(({\mathcal{F}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))|_{U_{\sigma}})=\chi^{-m_{\sigma}}\cdot\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{F}}|_{U_{\sigma}}).

∎

Example 4.4
.

Generalizing the case of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, by
[
16
]
[Prop.8.2.18], one has for any
0\leq p\leq n=\dim(X)
,

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma}{\dim(O_{\tau})\choose p}\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}.

Hence,

(150)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(\omega_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}.

In particular, if
X
is quasi-projective, then

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma}(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\tau})}\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y].

If, moreover,
\sigma
is a smooth cone with
m_{\sigma,i}
,
i=1,\ldots,n
, the minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
, then

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)|_{U_{\sigma}})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+(1+y)\cdot\sum_{k\geq 1}(\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}})^{k}\right),

hence

(151)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)|_{U_{\sigma}}))=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+y\cdot\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}{1-\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}.

Consider now the case of a simplicial cone
\sigma
with minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
as before, and let
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}=\Omega^{p}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}
,
p\geq 0
, resp.,
[{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}]=mC^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{y}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma})=\sum_{p=0}^{n}[\Omega^{p}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}]\cdot y^{p}\in K_{0}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma})[y]
. Then

tr_{\sigma}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(mC^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{y}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma}))=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+(1+y)\cdot\sum_{k\geq 1}(a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}})^{k}\right).

By applying diagram (
145
) to
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}=\Omega^{p}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}
, with
{\mathcal{F}}=\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}(\Omega^{p}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}})\simeq\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}}
, resp.,
[{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}]=mC^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{y}(U^{\prime}_{\sigma})
, with
[{\mathcal{F}}]=mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)|_{U_{\sigma}}
,
we get

(152)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)|_{U_{\sigma}}))=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+y\cdot a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}\in{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}.

Finally, one can twist the sheaves
\widehat{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
by
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)
, for
D
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor, and use (
149
) to get the corresponding identities. ∎

Remark 4.5
.

If, more generally, we work with
X=X_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
a closed
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant algebraic subset of
X_{\Sigma}
corresponding to a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
, similar localization formulas hold for the sheaves
\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}
of Ishida
p
-forms of
X
(extended by zero to
X_{\Sigma}
). More precisely, using now
[
3
]
[Sect.3], one gets for
0\leq p\leq\dim(X)

(153)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(\widetilde{\Omega}^{p}_{X}|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{\prime}}{\dim(O_{\tau})\choose p}\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}.

In particular, if
X=V_{\tau}\subset X_{\Sigma}
is the orbit closure for
\tau\preceq\sigma
of dimension
d
, then
\widetilde{\Omega}^{d}_{X}=\omega_{V_{\tau}}
, so we get by (
99
) that

(154)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC_{0}([O_{\tau}\hookrightarrow X_{\Sigma}])|_{U_{\sigma}})=\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(\omega_{V_{\tau}}|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}.

Example 4.6
.

Let
Z=X_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
be a closed
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant algebraic subset of
X=X_{\Sigma}
corresponding to a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
, with open complement
V:=X\setminus Z
. Then

(155)

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([V\hookrightarrow X])|_{U_{\sigma}})=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma;\tau\notin\Sigma^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\tau})}\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{\rm sum}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y].

We now consider the case when
Z=D_{K}:=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}D_{\rho}
, for
K\subset\Sigma(1)
, and
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
is a smooth, resp., simplicial cone.

Let us first assume that
\sigma
is smooth, with
m_{\sigma,i}
,
i=1,\ldots,n
, the minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
. Then

\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([V\hookrightarrow X])|_{U_{\sigma}})\\
=\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\left((1+y)\cdot\sum_{k\geq 1}(\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}})^{k}\right)\cdot\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\left(1+(1+y)\cdot\sum_{k\geq 1}(\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}})^{k}\right),

hence

(156)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([V\hookrightarrow X])|_{U_{\sigma}}))=\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{(1+y)\cdot\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}{1-\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1+y\cdot\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}{1-\chi^{-m_{\sigma,i}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}.

Consider now the case of a simplicial cone
\sigma
with minimal generators of
\sigma^{\vee}
as before, and let
V^{\prime}=U^{\prime}_{\sigma}\setminus D_{K^{\prime}}
, with

D_{K^{\prime}}=\bigcup_{\rho\in K^{\prime}}D^{\prime}_{\rho}
, for
K^{\prime}:=\sigma(1)\cap K
. Let
[{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}]=mC^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{y}([V^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U^{\prime}_{\sigma}])
. Then

tr_{\sigma}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(mC^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{y}([V\hookrightarrow U^{\prime}_{\sigma}]))=\\
\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{\rho_{i}\in K^{\prime}}\left((1+y)\sum_{k\geq 1}(a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}})^{k}\right)\cdot\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K^{\prime}}\left(1+(1+y)\sum_{k\geq 1}(a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}})^{k}\right).

By applying (
145
) to
[{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}]=mC^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{y}([V^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U^{\prime}_{\sigma}])
, with
[{\mathcal{F}}]=\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}])=mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([V\hookrightarrow X])|_{U_{\sigma}}
,
we get

(157)

\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}(mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}([V\hookrightarrow X])|_{U_{\sigma}})=\\
\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}\cdot\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1+y\cdot a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot\chi^{-m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}}}.

∎

We now explain the following equality, originally due to Brion
[
9
]
, where we follow the approach of
[
13
]
[Cor.1.3] (see also
[
16
]
[Thm.13.2.8] for a special case):

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be a complete toric variety of complex dimension
n
, with
{\mathcal{F}}
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf on
X
. Then the
K
-theoretic Euler characteristic of
{\mathcal{F}}
can be calculated via localization at the
{\mathbb{T}}
-fixed points as:

(158)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\mathbb{S}(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{F}}|_{U_{\sigma}}))\in\mbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}[M]_{S}.

Proof.

Let
x_{\sigma}=O_{\sigma}\subset U_{\sigma}
be the torus fixed point corresponding to
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
. The assertion follows from the commutativity of the lower right square of the following diagram:

\begin{CD}\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(x_{\sigma})@>{i_{*}}>{}>K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)@>{\chi^{\mathbb{T}}}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(x_{\sigma})_{S}@>{\sim}>{}>K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)_{S}@>{\chi^{\mathbb{T}}}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}\\
\Big\|@V{\wr}V{}V\Big\|\\
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(x_{\sigma})_{S}@>{\sim}>{}>\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(U_{\sigma})_{S}@>{\sum_{\sigma}(\mathbb{S}\circ\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}})}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}\>.\end{CD}

The vertical maps are the natural localization maps (upon restriction to the
U_{\sigma}
’s for the middle map). The commutativity of the outer square follows from (
143
). The lower left square commutes by the functoriality of restriction and localization. This yields the desired commutativity of the lower right square.
∎

As a consequence, we get the following weighted version of Brion’s equality
[
9
]
[page 655], see also
[
16
]
[Cor.13.2.10(a)].

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
P
be a full-dimensional lattice polytope with associated projective toric variety
X=X_{P}
and ample Cartier divisor
D=D_{P}
. For each vertex
v
of
P
, consider the cone
C_{v}={\rm Cone}(P\cap M-v)=\sigma_{v}^{\vee}
, with faces
E_{v}={\rm Cone}(E\cap M-v)
for
v\in E
.
Then the following identity holds in
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y]
:

(159)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\sum_{v\ \text{\rm vertex}}\chi^{-v}\cdot\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{v\in E\preceq P}(1+y)^{\dim(E)}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E_{v})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\right).

Proof.

By the definition of the inner normal fan
\Sigma_{P}
of
P
, one has for any vertex
v
of
P
that
\sigma_{v}=C_{v}^{\vee}
, with
C_{v}
defined as above (see
[
16
]
[page 76]). By dualizing, it follows that
C_{v}=\sigma_{v}^{\vee}
(cf.
[
16
]
[Prop.1.2.4]). By
[
16
]
[Prop.1.2.10], the faces
E_{v}
of
C_{v}
are exactly of the form
\sigma_{v}^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp}
, with
\tau\preceq\sigma_{v}
. Note also that the Cartier data for
D=D_{P}
over
U_{\sigma_{v}}
is exactly given by
m_{\sigma_{v}}=v\in M
, see
[
16
]
[(4.2.8)].

The desired identity (
159
) follows now from Theorem
4.7
together with Example
4.4
and formula (
149
).
∎

Remark 4.9
.

A direct calculation of
\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))
, without using localization techniques, can be obtained from
[
16
]
[Lem.9.4.8 and Thm.9.3.1], as follows:

(160)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{y}(X)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\sum_{E\preceq P}(1+y)^{\dim(E)}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E)\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y].

This is a
K
-theoretic version of Corollary
3.15
. For
y=0
, formula (
160
) reduces to

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\sum_{m\in P\cap M}\chi^{-m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[M],

which can already be deduced from (
38
) and (
39
).

For
y=0
, formula (
159
) therefore reduces to Brion’s equality:

(161)

\sum_{m\in P\cap M}\chi^{-m}=\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D))=\sum_{v\ \text{\rm vertex}}\chi^{-v}\cdot\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in C_{v}\cap M}\chi^{-m}\right).

4.2.
Localization in equivariant homology

Let
X_{\Sigma}
be an
n
-dimensional toric variety with torus
{\mathbb{T}}=T_{N}
.
Let
X=X_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed algebraic subset of
X_{\Sigma}
, defined by a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
,
such that the fixed-point set
X^{\mathbb{T}}\neq\emptyset
.

By
[
15
]
[Lem.8.4, Lem.8.5], the inclusion
i:X^{\mathbb{T}}\hookrightarrow X
induces an injective morphism of
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})
-modules

i_{*}:\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{Q}})\hookrightarrow\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})

which becomes an isomorphism

i_{*}:\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\simeq\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}

upon localization at the multiplicative subset
L\subset(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}=H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by the elements
\pm c(m)
, for
0\neq m\in M
(cf.
[
15
]
[Cor.8.9]).

Remark 4.10
.

In particular, by applying the localization isomorphism to both
X
and
X_{\Sigma}
, it follows that the homomorphism
\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\to\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{\Sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
induced by inclusion is injective. So in the following it suffices to work in the localized homology
\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{\Sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
of the ambient toric variety.

Let us now assume that
X=X_{\Sigma}
. Since

\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus_{x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}}\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(x;{\mathbb{Q}}),

one gets via the isomorphism
i_{*}
a projection map of
H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})
-modules, called here the
homological localization map at
x
,

pr_{x}:\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\simeq\bigoplus_{x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}}\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(x;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\longrightarrow\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(x;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}=L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Remark 4.11
.

If
X
is a toric variety with
{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
a finitely generated free
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
-module (e.g.,
X
is a complete simplicial toric variety, see
[
16
]
[Prop.12.4.7]), then the localization map

\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\to\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
is injective.

The equivariant Todd class transformation
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}
commutes with pushforward for closed inclusions
i_{*}
and pullback
j^{*}
under open inclusions. So
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}
is compatible with the two diagrams below:

(162)

\begin{CD}K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X^{\mathbb{T}})@>{i_{*}}>{}>K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\widehat{H}_{2*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{Q}})@>{i_{*}}>{}>\widehat{H}_{2*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{j^{*}}V@>{{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}}>{}>@V{}V{}V@V{}V{j^{*}}V\\
K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(x_{\sigma})@>{i_{*}}>{}>K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(U_{\sigma})\widehat{H}_{2*}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})@>{i_{*}}>{}>\widehat{H}_{2*}^{\mathbb{T}}(U_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})\end{CD}

To extend this property to the localized versions, we need to show that the ring homomorphism
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}:{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\simeq K_{\mathbb{T}}^{0}(pt)\to(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
given by the equivariant Chern character is compatible with the multiplicative subsets used for localization. This fact follows as in
[
16
]
[pag.644]; indeed, if
0\neq m\in M
, then

{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}(1-\chi^{m})=1-e^{c(m)}=c(m)\frac{1-e^{c(m)}}{c(m)}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>,

with
c(m)\in L
and the
convergent
power series
\frac{1-e^{c(m)}}{c(m)}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
being invertible (since its constant coefficient is a unit). So
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}
induces a ring homomorphism of localized rings

{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}:{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}\simeq K_{\mathbb{T}}^{0}(pt)_{S}\longrightarrow L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>.

In particular, this localized version of the equivariant Chern character
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}
can be applied to the image of the summation map
\mathbb{S}:{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]_{\rm Sum}\to{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}
, where by abuse of notation we denote

({\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}\circ\mathbb{S})\left(\sum_{m\in M}a_{m}\cdot\chi^{m}\right)=:\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in M}a_{m}\cdot e^{c(m)}\right),

extending the corresponding formula
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}(\sum_{m\in M}a_{m}\cdot\chi^{m})=\sum_{m\in M}a_{m}\cdot e^{c(m)}
for finite sums in
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]\subset{\mathbb{Z}}[[M]]_{\rm Sum}
.

Remark 4.12
.

In the context of diagram (
145
), we have to also consider the extension of the Chern character to the complexified
K
-theory

{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}:{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]\simeq K_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}^{0}(pt)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{C}}\to(\Lambda^{an}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}},

which is compatible with localization

{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}:{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}\simeq K_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}^{0}(pt)_{S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow{L^{\prime}}^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset{L^{\prime}}^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}\>.

Here,
S^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]
is as before the multiplicative subset generated by elements
1-a\cdot\chi^{m^{\prime}}
, for
0\neq m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
and
a\in{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
, and
L^{\prime}\subset(\Lambda_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}=H^{*}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(pt;{\mathbb{C}})
is the multiplicative set generated by the elements
\pm a\cdot c(m^{\prime})
, for
0\neq m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
and
a\in{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. The compatibility with localization follows as above, using in addition that

{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(1-a\cdot\chi^{m^{\prime}})=1-a\cdot e^{c(m^{\prime})}\in(\Lambda^{an}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}\>,

for
a\neq 1
and
0\neq m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
, is an invertible convergent power series since its constant coefficient
1-a
is a unit.
These two Chern characters
{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}
and
{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}}
fit into a commutative diagram

(163)

\begin{CD}{\mathbb{C}}[M^{\prime}]_{S^{\prime}}@>{{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}}>{}>{L^{\prime}}^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}@>{}>{}>{L^{\prime}}^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
{\mathbb{Z}}[M]_{S}@>{{\rm ch}^{{\mathbb{T}}}}>{}>{L}^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{{\mathbb{T}}})_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{}>{}>{L}^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{{\mathbb{T}}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\ .\end{CD}

The last two vertical arrows are injections (between convergent, resp., formal Laurent series with rational or complex coefficients).

By the module property (
81
) of the equivariant Todd class transformation and the functoriality of
{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}
under pullbacks, it follows that
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}
is compatible with the corresponding localized versions of the diagrams appearing in (
162
), with the corresponding maps labeled
i_{*}
being now isomorphisms, as used in the definitions of the localization maps
pr_{x}
in
K
-theory and homology, respectively.

Proposition 4.13
.

For a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
on
X=X_{\Sigma}
, and
x_{\sigma}\in X^{\mathbb{T}}
a given fixed point of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action, we have:

(164)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])_{x_{\sigma}}:=pr_{x_{\sigma}}({\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])={\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}((\mathbb{S}\circ\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}})({\mathcal{F}}))\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and

(165)

\begin{split}L^{-1}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])&=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])_{x_{\sigma}}\cdot[x_{\sigma}]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\\
&=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}{\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}((\mathbb{S}\circ\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}})({\mathcal{F}}))\cdot[x_{\sigma}]_{{\mathbb{T}}}\in\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.\end{split}

Proof.

Indeed, using the explicit calculation of the localization map in equivariant
K
-theory (as in the previous subsection), we have:

pr_{x_{\sigma}}({\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])={\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(pr_{x_{\sigma}}([{\mathcal{F}}]))={\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}((\mathbb{S}\circ\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}})({\mathcal{F}})).

The second formula just follows from the isomorphism
i_{*}:\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\simeq\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
.
∎

Example 4.14

(Localized equivariant Hirzebruch and Todd classes)
.

Assume now that
X_{\Sigma}
is a quasi-projective toric variety. Let, moreover,
X:=X_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
be the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant closed algebraic subset defined by a star-closed subset
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
, with
x_{\sigma}\in X^{\mathbb{T}}
a given fixed point of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-action. By using (
164
) and (
153
), the following identity holds in
L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y]\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y]
:

(166)

\begin{split}T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)_{x_{\sigma}}&:={\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)])_{x_{\sigma}}=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\tau})}\cdot({\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}\circ\mathbb{S})\left(\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}\chi^{-m}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\tau})}\cdot\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\>.\end{split}

We thus have in
\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[y]
(compare also with
[
42
]
[Thm.5.3],
[
44
]
[Thm.11.3]):

(167)

L^{-1}T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}(X)=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{\prime}(n)}\left(\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(O_{\tau})}\cdot\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\right)\cdot[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}}.

Specializing the above formulae to
y=0
for
X
quasi-projective, or applying (
164
) to the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
(extended by zero to the ambient toric variety, which now can be arbitrary) we get the following result:

(168)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)_{x_{\sigma}}=\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{\prime}}\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>,

and

(169)

L^{-1}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{\prime}(n)}\left(\sum_{\tau\preceq\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{\prime}}\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\right)\cdot[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

In the case
\Sigma^{\prime}=\Sigma
, formulas (
168
) and (
169
) reduce to
a result of Brylinski-Zhang
[
15
]
[Thm.9.4]:

(170)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{\Sigma})_{x_{\sigma}}=\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>,

and

(171)

L^{-1}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{\Sigma})=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right)\cdot[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X_{\Sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

∎

Example 4.15

(Localized equivariant Hirzebruch classes for complements of divisors)
.

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be a toric variety with
V:=X\setminus D_{K}
, and
D_{K}=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}D_{\rho}
for
K\subset\Sigma(1)
. Let
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
be a simplicial cone with
x_{\sigma}\in X^{\mathbb{T}}
the corresponding
{\mathbb{T}}
-fixed point.
Then we get by (
157
) and Proposition
4.13
the following identity in

{L^{\prime}}^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}[y]\subset{L^{\prime}}^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{C}}[y]
:

(172)

T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([V\hookrightarrow X])_{x_{\sigma}}:={\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([V\hookrightarrow X])])_{x_{\sigma}}\\
=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})}}\cdot\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1+y\cdot a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})}}.

In particular, if
\sigma
is a smooth cone, then

\begin{split}T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([V\hookrightarrow X])_{x_{\sigma}}=\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{(1+y)\cdot e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}}{1-e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}}\cdot\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1+y\cdot e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}}{1-e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}},\end{split}

with
m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i}=m_{\sigma,i}
for all
i
(since
\sigma
is a smooth cone).

By specializing (
172
) to
y=0
, we get a local version of the Todd type formula (
139
). In particular, for
y=0
and
\sigma
smooth, we get:

(173)

T^{\mathbb{T}}_{0*}([V\hookrightarrow X])_{x_{\sigma}}=\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}}{1-e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}}\cdot\prod_{\rho_{i}\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1}{1-e^{-c(m_{\sigma,i})}}.

In the case when
K=\emptyset
, we thus obtain a more explicit expression for the equivariant Hirzebruch and Todd classes of
X
localized at
x_{\sigma}
. ∎

As a consequence of Proposition
4.13
and Theorem
4.7
, we also have the following.

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be a complete toric variety of complex dimension
n
, with
{\mathcal{F}}
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-coherent sheaf on
X
. Then the cohomological Euler characteristic of
{\mathcal{F}}
can be calculated via localization at the
{\mathbb{T}}
-fixed points as:

(174)

\begin{split}\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{F}})&=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}({\rm ch}^{\mathbb{T}}\circ\mathbb{S})(\chi_{\sigma}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{F}}|_{U_{\sigma}}))\\
&=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])_{x_{\sigma}}\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>.\end{split}

Before giving a more classical description of the homological localization map
pr_{x}
in the context of simplicial toric varieties, let us formulate the following compatibility of
pr_{x}:\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\to\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(x;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
with cap products. As before,
X=X_{\Sigma}
is a toric variety, with
x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}
, and let
i_{x}:\{x\}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion map. Then, for any
a\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
and
b\in\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
, we have:

(175)

pr_{x}(a\cap b)=i_{x}^{*}(a)\cap pr_{x}(b).

This follows from the definition of
pr_{x}
, since the cap product
\cap
commutes with pullback for open inclusions, together with the projection formula for a closed inclusion.

For the rest of this subsection we assume that
X=X_{\Sigma}
is a
simplicial
toric variety, so that one has equivariant Poincaré duality with
{\mathbb{Q}}
-coefficients (recall that in the non-compact case,
{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}
denotes equivariant Borel-Moore homology):

\cap[X]_{\mathbb{T}}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

For this reason, in the following we use interchangeably homology and cohomology, with a cap product in homology corresponding to multiplication in cohomology.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be a simplicial toric variety of dimension
n
, with
x_{\sigma}\in X^{\mathbb{T}}
a torus fixed point and inclusion map
i_{\sigma}:\{x_{\sigma}\}\hookrightarrow X
. Then

(176)

pr_{x_{\sigma}}=\frac{i_{\sigma}^{*}}{Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L},

with the generalized Euler class of the fixed point
x_{\sigma}
in
X
defined by

0\neq Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}):=i_{\sigma}^{*}\left({\rm mult}(\sigma)\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}\right)\in{\mathbb{Q}}\cdot L.

If
X
is, moreover, a complete simplicial toric variety, let

\int_{X}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})_{L}\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})_{L}=L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the equivariant Gysin map (or, equivalently, the equivariant pushforward) for the constant map
X\to pt
. Then

(177)

\int_{X}=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{i_{\sigma}^{*}}{Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

Proof.

For
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
a cone of maximal dimension, formula (
62
) written in homological terms
becomes:

\left({\rm mult}(\sigma)\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}\right)\cap[X]_{\mathbb{T}}={(i_{\sigma})}_{*}[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}}.

Using (
175
), we get

i_{\sigma}^{*}\left({\rm mult}(\sigma)\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}\right)\cap pr_{x_{\sigma}}([X]_{\mathbb{T}})=pr_{x_{\sigma}}({(i_{\sigma})}_{*}[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}})=[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}},

where the last equality follows from the fact that the composition
pr_{x_{\sigma}}\circ{(i_{\sigma})}_{*}
is by definition just the identity. The factor
i_{\sigma}^{*}\left({\rm mult}(\sigma)\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}\right)
is by definition the generalized Euler class
0\neq Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})

(this is non-zero by the formula above, since
[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}}\neq 0
), and it is an element in
{\mathbb{Q}}\cdot L
(see
[
16
]
[Lem..13.3.5]) since any divisor on a simplicial toric variety is
{\mathbb{Q}}
-Cartier (
[
16
]
[Prop.4.2.7]). So formula (
176
) follows now from the projection formula (
175
), i.e., for
a\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
, we have:

pr_{x_{\sigma}}(a\cap[X]_{\mathbb{T}})=i_{\sigma}^{*}(a)\cap\frac{[x_{\sigma}]_{\mathbb{T}}}{Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})}.

For the second assertion, note that the map

\int_{X}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})_{L}\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})_{L}=L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

corresponds (by the functoriality of pushforward) under the localization isomorphism

\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\simeq\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X^{\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}=\bigoplus_{x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}}\widehat{H}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(x;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}

to the sum of localization maps
pr_{x}
,
x\in X^{\mathbb{T}}
, i.e.,

\int_{X}=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}pr_{x_{\sigma}}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

∎

Remark 4.18
.

If the fixed point
x_{\sigma}
corresponds to a smooth cone
\sigma\in\Sigma
, then
{\rm mult}(\sigma)=1
and the divisors
D_{\rho}
(
\rho\in\sigma(1)
) are Cartier divisors. Moreover, the normal bundle

N_{x_{\sigma}}U_{\sigma}
of
x_{\sigma}
in the smooth affine variety
U_{\sigma}
is given by

N_{x_{\sigma}}U_{\sigma}=\bigoplus_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}{\mathcal{O}}(D_{\rho})|_{x_{\sigma}}.

Hence
Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})=c^{\mathbb{T}}_{n}(N_{x_{\sigma}}U_{\sigma})
is the classical equivariant Euler class of the fixed point
x_{\sigma}
, given by the top equivariant Chern class of the normal bundle
N_{x_{\sigma}}U_{\sigma}
.

Translating Theorem
4.7
into the (co)homological context, using Proposition
4.13
and, in the simplicial context,
Proposition
4.17
, we get the following consequence.

Corollary 4.19
.

Let
X=X_{\Sigma}
be a complete toric variety,
D
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant Cartier divisor, with
{\mathcal{F}}
a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf on
X
. Then the cohomological equivariant Euler characteristic of
{\mathcal{F}}
is computed by

(178)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}})=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}pr_{x_{\sigma}}({\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}}])\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

If, moreover,
X
is a simplicial toric variety, then

(179)

\chi^{\mathbb{T}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}})=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{i_{\sigma}^{*}(e^{[D]_{\mathbb{T}}}{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}]))}{Eu_{X}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma})}\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

4.3.
Equivariant Hirzebruch classes of simplicial toric varieties via localization

In this subsection, we explain how to reprove Theorems
3.22
,
3.28
and Lemma
3.29

by localization techniques, instead of using the equivariant Lefschetz-Riemann-Roch theorem of
[
24
]
[Thm.3.1 and Rem.4.3] (as done in Subsection
3.3
).

Let
X:=X_{\Sigma}
be a simplicial quasi-projective toric variety associated to a fan
\Sigma
of full-dimensional convex support. Then the equivariant cohomology of
X
is a free
(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
-module (by (
63
)), so that the injectivity part of the sequence (
65
), resp., (
66
), still holds for the equivariant cohomology (as pointed out in Remark
2.8
). Hence the corresponding map for the completed equivariant cohomology rings is injective, i.e.,

\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}i_{\sigma}^{*}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}}).

By localizing at the multiplicative set
L
, and using the exactness of localization, we get an injective map

\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}i_{\sigma}^{*}:\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

So it is enough to check these characteristic class formulae by using for each fixed point
x_{\sigma}
,
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
, the induced restriction map
pr_{x_{\sigma}}

(180)

\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\overset{pr_{x_{\sigma}}}{\to}\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{C}})_{L^{\prime}},

with the middle arrow

pr_{x_{\sigma}}
as in (
176
).
Even the direct sum
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}pr_{x_{\sigma}}
of these induced restriction maps is still injective, since the localization map on the integral domain
\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})\to\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}
is injective, and
pr_{x_{\sigma}}=\frac{i_{\sigma}^{*}}{Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})}

differs from
i_{\sigma}^{*}
by the unit

Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})=i_{\sigma}^{*}\left({\rm mult}(\sigma)\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}\right)=|G_{\sigma}|\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{Q}})_{L}.

Moreover, as mentioned after diagram (
163
), no information is lost if we consider complex instead of rational coefficients.

4.3.1.
Proof of Lemma
3.29
by localization

In order to reprove formula (
138
) by localization, we start with the local version discussed in the following example.

Example 4.20
.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
be a simplicial cone with minimal generators
\{m_{\sigma,i}\}_{i}
of
\sigma^{\vee}
as in the context of diagram (
145
). For
{\mathcal{F}}:=\pi_{*}^{G_{\sigma}}({\mathcal{O}}_{U^{\prime}_{\sigma}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime}}})
with
m^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
, we get by Proposition
4.13
and formula (
148
) the identity

(181)

{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}([{\mathcal{F}}])_{x_{\sigma}}=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\chi^{m^{\prime}}(g)e^{c(m^{\prime})}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}(x_{\sigma};{\mathbb{C}})_{L^{\prime}}.

∎

Using Example
4.20
, we can now reprove formula (
138
) of Lemma
3.29
by localization techniques. Recall that the global formula

(182)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(\pi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{W}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{\widetilde{m}}})^{G})=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\chi^{\widetilde{m}}(g^{-1})\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot e^{\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}}),

with
\{e_{\rho}\}_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}
the standard basis of the lattice
\widetilde{N}={\mathbb{Z}}^{|\Sigma(1)|}
and
\widetilde{m}
in the dual character lattice
\widetilde{M}
, is formulated in terms of the Cox construction
\pi:W\to X
.
So let us now compare, for a fixed
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
with
U_{\sigma}
the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant open affine subset of
X
containing the corresponding fixed point
x_{\sigma}
, the local and global formulae (
181
) and, resp., (
182
).

To simplify the notation, assume
\sigma(1)=\{\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{n}\}
. We next adapt the arguments of
[
13
]
[page 24] to our context. If
g\in G_{\Sigma}\setminus G_{\sigma}
, by (
31
) there exists a
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
with
a_{\rho}(g)\neq 1
, so that the restriction of

\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot e^{\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}}
to
U_{\sigma}
becomes
0
. Thus, the summation on the right-hand side of (
182
) reduces after restriction to
U_{\sigma}
to a summation over
{g\in G_{\sigma}}
. If
g\in G_{\sigma}
and
\rho\notin\sigma(1)
, then
a_{\rho}(g)=1
, so that the restriction of

\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot e^{\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}}
to
U_{\sigma}
becomes
1
. So the product on the right hand side of (
182
) reduces to a product over
\rho\in\sigma(1)
.
As in the proof of formula (
125
), we have

\pi^{-1}(U_{\sigma})=U^{\prime}_{\sigma}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
, with
\widetilde{{\mathbb{T}}}\simeq{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
,
G\simeq G_{\sigma}\times({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
, and
U^{\prime}_{\sigma}/G_{\sigma}=U_{\sigma}
. Let
\widetilde{m}=m^{\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}\in M^{\prime}\oplus M^{\prime\prime}
be the corresponding character decomposition. Note that
{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{m^{\prime\prime}}}
is a trivial
({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
-equivariant line bundle on
({\mathbb{C}}^{*})^{r-n}
, so we can assume that
m^{\prime\prime}=0
is the trivial character. By the projection formula, the restriction of
\pi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{W}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{\chi^{\widetilde{m}}})^{G}
to
U_{\sigma}
is isomorphic to the
{\mathcal{F}}
appearing in (
181
). Finally,

\displaystyle pr_{x_{\sigma}}\left(\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\chi^{\widetilde{m}}(g^{-1})\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot e^{\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Eu_{X}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma})}\cdot{i_{\sigma}^{*}}\left(\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\chi^{\widetilde{m}}(g^{-1})\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot e^{\langle\widetilde{m},e_{\rho}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)e^{-F_{\rho}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Eu_{X}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma})}\cdot{i_{\sigma}^{*}}\left(\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\chi^{m^{\prime}}(g^{-1})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{F_{\rho_{i}}\cdot e^{\langle{m^{\prime}},e_{\rho_{i}}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho_{i}}}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g)e^{-F_{\rho_{i}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\chi^{m^{\prime}}(g^{-1})\cdot i_{\sigma}^{*}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{e^{\langle{m^{\prime}},e_{\rho_{i}}\rangle\cdot F_{\rho_{i}}}}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g)e^{-F_{\rho_{i}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\chi^{m^{\prime}}(g)e^{c(m^{\prime})}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-a_{\rho_{i}}(g^{-1})\cdot e^{-c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})}},

where the last equality uses (
67
) for the torus
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
to show that
i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho_{i}}=c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})
and
c(m^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle m^{\prime},e_{\rho_{i}}\rangle i^{*}_{\sigma}F_{\rho_{i}}
, as well as changing
g
by
g^{-1}
in
G_{\sigma}
. Also note that
a_{\rho}(g)
for
g\in G_{\Sigma}
and
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
fits with the corresponding
a_{\rho}(g)
for
g\in G_{\sigma}
and
\rho\in\sigma(1)
, as in (
33
).

Altogether, the local formula (
181
) is obtained from the global formula (
182
) upon applying the homological localization map
pr_{x_{\sigma}}
of (
180
). ∎

4.3.2.
Proof of Theorem
3.28
by localization

We can now explain a proof of formula (
136
)
of Theorem
3.28
via localization. With

X:=X_{\Sigma}
as before,
let
V\subset X
be the open complement of the divisor
D_{K}:=\bigcup_{\rho\in K}D_{\rho}
, for
K\subset\Sigma(1)
.
Recall that the equivariant Hirzebruch class
{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([V\hookrightarrow X])
is computed by:

(183)

{T}^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([V\hookrightarrow X])=(1+y)^{n-r}\cdot\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\prod_{\rho\notin K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\>,

with
r=|\Sigma(1)|
, and
F_{\rho}=[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}
the equivariant fundamental class of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor
D_{\rho}
corresponding to the ray
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
. As above, it suffices to prove the equality of both sides of formula
(
183
) after applying the homological localization map
pr_{x_{\sigma}}
of (
180
), with
pr_{x_{\sigma}}(T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([V\hookrightarrow X]))=T^{\mathbb{T}}_{y*}([V\hookrightarrow X])_{x_{\sigma}}
computed by formula
(
172
).

If
g\in G_{\Sigma}\setminus G_{\sigma}
, by (
31
) there exists a
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
with
a_{\rho}(g)\neq 1
, so that one factor on the restriction of the right-hand side of (
183
) to
U_{\sigma}
becomes
0
. Hence the summation on the right-hand side of (
183
) reduces after restriction to
U_{\sigma}
to a summation over
{g\in G_{\sigma}}
. If
g\in G_{\sigma}
and
\rho\notin\sigma(1)
, then
a_{\rho}(g)=1
, so that the restriction of the factor
\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}
(for
\rho\in K
)
or
\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}
(for
\rho\notin K
) to
U_{\sigma}
becomes
1+y
. With
r-n=|\Sigma(1)\setminus\sigma(1)|
, this will in turn cancel the factor
(1+y)^{n-r}
of formula (
183
).
Therefore, we get

\displaystyle pr_{x_{\sigma}}\left((1+y)^{n-r}\cdot\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\prod_{\rho\notin K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Eu_{X}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma})}\cdot{i_{\sigma}^{*}}\left(\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{F_{\rho}\cdot\big(1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}\big)}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|G_{\sigma}|}\sum_{g\in G_{\sigma}}{i_{\sigma}^{*}}\left(\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{(1+y)\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\prod_{\rho\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1+y\cdot a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-F_{\rho}}}\right).

This expression reduces to formula (
172
), as desired, after
using (
67
) for the torus
{\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
to show that for
\rho=\rho_{i}
the rays of
\sigma(1)
, one gets as before
i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho_{i}}=c(m^{\prime}_{\sigma,i})
, as well as by changing
g
by
g^{-1}
in
G_{\sigma}
.∎

Remark 4.21
.

Specializing to
K=\emptyset
, the above arguments also reprove formula (
126
) of Theorem
3.22
.

5.
Euler-Maclaurin formulae via equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch

An Euler-Maclaurin formula relates the sum
\sum_{m\in P\cap M}f(m)
of the values of a suitable function
f
at the lattice points in a polyhedron
P\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}:=M\otimes\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}
to integrals over suitable polyhedra. Here, we are interested in the case where
P
is a polytope or a cone. The corresponding results for pointed cones will fit with the localization results developed in the previous section.

We begin this section with a short overview in Subsection
5.1
of relations between the equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch theorem and Euler-Maclaurin type formulae obtained via polytope dilations, as indicated in works of Khovanskii-Pukhlikov
[
38
]
and Brion-Vergne
[
13
]
.

In Subsection
5.2
we extend the approach of Brion-Vergne
[
13
]
by allowing arbitrary
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant coherent sheaf coefficients. We derive an abstract Euler-Maclaurin type formula (even for exponential functions instead of polynomials),
based on the equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch theorem and the motivic formalism of equivariant characteristic classes developed in the previous sections. To deal with various convergence issues in the context of exponential functions, we introduce and use an analytic version of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant cohomology ring.

The abstract Euler-Maclaurin formula is specialized in Subsection
5.3
to various situations, recovering many known Euler-Maclaurin type formulae as well as obtaining several new ones in a uniform way dictated by toric geometry.

5.1.
Brief overview of Euler-Maclaurin formulae

The theory of valuations for rational polyhedra
P\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
was applied by
Khovanskii and Pukhlikov in
[
38
]
to obtain Euler-Maclaurin type formulae for Delzant polytopes
(corresponding to smooth projective toric varieties) in terms of infinitesimal movements
P(h)
of the polytope. More precisely, for a polynomial
f
one has the identity:

(184)

Todd(\frac{\partial}{\partial h})\left(\int_{P(h)}f(m)\ dm\right)_{|_{h=0}}=\sum_{m\in P\cap M}f(m)\>.

Here, if the polytope
P
is the lattice polytope defined by inequalities of the form

\langle m,u_{F}\rangle+c_{F}\geq 0,

with
u_{F}
the facet normals, the polytope
P(h)
with shifted faces is defined by inequalities

\langle m,u_{F}\rangle+c_{F}+h_{F}\geq 0,

with
h=(h_{F})_{F}
a vector with real entries indexed by the facets
F
of
P
. Moreover,
Todd(\frac{\partial}{\partial h})
is the differential operator defined by:

Todd(\frac{\partial}{\partial h}):=\prod_{F\ {\rm facet}}\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial h_{F}}}{1-e^{-\frac{\partial}{\partial h_{F}}}}.

The relation between formula (
184
) and the equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch theorem is only indicated in
[
38
]
,
but not used (but see, e.g., the proof of
[
16
]
[Thm.13.5.6]). Moreover, it is clearly pointed out that exponential functions
f(x)=e^{\langle x,z\rangle}
are needed to work with rational polyhedral cones. From this, one then gets the result for polynomials
f
(compare also with
[
16
]
[Sect.13.5]).

For a direct extension of (
184
) to simple lattice polytopes, with a corresponding more complicated Todd operator

Todd(\frac{\partial}{\partial h})
, see
[
12
]
as well as the nice survey
[
34
]
.
The approach to the Euler-Maclaurin formula (
184
) through the equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch theorem
for projective simplicial toric varieties corresponding to simple lattice polytopes
is due to Brion-Vergne, see
[
13
]
[Thm.4.5].

5.2.
Euler-Maclaurin formulae via polytope dilations

In the following, we explain a uniform approach to various Euler-Maclaurin formulae for simple lattice polytopes or pointed cones, formulated in terms of dilations of the polytope or cone.
To have a unified language, assume
P
is a full-dimensional simple lattice polyhedron in
M_{\mathbb{R}}
with associated (semi-projective) toric variety
X=X_{P}
and inner normal fan
\Sigma=\Sigma_{P}
.
Let
P(h)
be the dilation of
P
with respect to the vector
h=(h_{\rho})_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}
with real entries indexed by the rays of
\Sigma
(as we use the related toric geometry), or equivalently, by the facets of
P
(if one would like to formulate everything directly in terms of
P
). So, if
P
is defined by inequalities of the form

\langle m,u_{\rho}\rangle+c_{\rho}\geq 0,

with
u_{\rho}
the ray generators and
c_{\rho}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, for each
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
, then
P(h)
is defined by inequalities

\langle m,u_{\rho}\rangle+c_{\rho}+h_{\rho}\geq 0,

for each
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
.
For later use in the context of weighted Euler-Maclaurin formulae, we also define a parametrized dilation
P_{y}(h)
of
P
by:

P_{y}(h):=\{m\in M\mid\langle m,u_{\rho}\rangle+(1+y)\cdot c_{\rho}+h_{\rho}\geq 0,\ {\rm for\ all}\ \rho\in\Sigma(1)\},

with
h=(h_{\rho})_{\rho}
as before a vector with real entries indexed by the rays
\rho
of
\Sigma
. Note that
P_{0}(h)=P(h)
, and
P_{y}(0)=:P_{y}
is the dilation of
P
by the factor
1+y
.

Let us first consider the case when
P
is a full-dimensional lattice polytope in
M_{\mathbb{R}}
.
In what follows, we adapt the arguments of Brion-Vergne
[
13
]
[Thm.4.5] to our context.
If
h
is in a small enough open neighborhood
U
of the origin in
{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
(with
r
the number of facets of
P
), then
P(h)
is again a simple polytope of the same combinatorial type, and similarly for
P_{y}(h)
.
Let us fix
h\in U\cap{\mathbb{Q}}^{r}
and
y\in{\mathbb{Q}}
, and choose
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
so that
k\cdot P_{y}(h)
is a lattice polytope in
M_{\mathbb{R}}
with ample Cartier divisor

D_{k\cdot P_{y}(h)}=k\cdot D_{P_{y}(h)}=k\left(\sum_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}((1+y)\cdot c_{\rho}+h_{\rho})\cdot D_{\rho}\right),

where
D_{P}=\sum_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}c_{\rho}\cdot D_{\rho}
is the ample Cartier divisor of
P
.
On a simplicial toric variety any divisor is
{\mathbb{Q}}
-Cartier (
[
16
]
[Prop.4.2.7]), so that
D_{P_{y}(h)}
has an equivariant fundamental class with rational coefficients satisfying
k\cdot[D_{P_{y}(h)}]_{\mathbb{T}}:=[k\cdot D_{P_{y}(h)}]_{\mathbb{T}}=c^{\mathbb{T}}_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(k\cdot D_{P_{y}(h)}))
, e.g., see
[
16
]
[Prop.12.4.13].
Applying formula (
84
) for
k\cdot P(h)
, and using the fact that the associated toric variety does not change under polytope dilation, we get

(185)

\sum_{m\in k\cdot P_{y}(h)\cap M}e^{s(m)}=\int_{X}e^{k\cdot[D_{P_{y}(h)}]_{\mathbb{T}}}\cap{\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X)\in(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>.

A localized version of formula (
185
) can be derived from (
177
) as:

(186)

\sum_{m\in k\cdot P_{y}(h)\cap M}e^{s(m)}=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{i_{\sigma}^{*}\left(e^{k\cdot[D_{P_{y}(h)}]_{\mathbb{T}}}\right)}{Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma})}\cdot i_{\sigma}^{*}({\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X))\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset L^{-1}(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\>.

Recall the identification

s:Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}(M)\simeq H_{\mathbb{T}}^{*}(pt;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})=:(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$}}
. Let
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}:=N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{C}}={\rm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{R}}(M_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}})
be given. By the universal property of
Sym
,
z
induces
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebra homomorphisms

\langle-,z\rangle:Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}}(M)\to{\mathbb{C}}\>,

by which we can view
\langle p,z\rangle
for
z
now variable and
p\in Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}}(M)
, resp.,
p\in(Sym_{\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}}(M))^{an}
fixed,
as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued polynomial on
N_{\mathbb{R}}
, resp., as a
convergent power series function
around zero in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Assume now that
z
is chosen so that
\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle\neq 0
for each
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
, i.e.,

\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},z\rangle\neq 0
, for each ray
\rho\in\sigma(1)
of
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
. Applying the function
\langle-,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle
to (
186
), we get

(187)

\sum_{m\in k\cdot P_{y}(h)\cap M}e^{\langle m,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{e^{\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}[D_{P_{y}(h)}]_{\mathbb{T}},z\rangle}}{\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}\cdot\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}({\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X)),\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle.

Note that by the Riemann sum approximation of an integral, we have:

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in k\cdot P_{y}(h)\cap M}e^{\langle m,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in P_{y}(h)\cap\frac{1}{k}M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\int_{P_{y}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm,

with the Lebesgue measure
dm
normalized so that the unit cube in
M\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
has volume
1
(which explains the use of the factor
\frac{1}{k^{n}}
).
We next study the limits on the right-hand side of (
187
). Note that

{k^{n}}\cdot\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle=\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle

since

\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle
is a (non-zero) homogeneous polynomial of degree
n
in
z
. Finally, we have that

(188)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}({\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X)),\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle=1.

Indeed, by formula (
130
), we have

\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}({\rm td}^{\mathbb{T}}_{*}(X)),\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle=\sum_{g\in G_{\Sigma}}\prod_{\rho\in\Sigma(1)}\frac{\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}F_{\rho},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}F_{\rho},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}},

with
F_{\rho}=[D_{\rho}]_{\mathbb{T}}
denoting the equivariant fundamental class of the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant divisor
D_{\rho}
corresponding to the ray
\rho\in\Sigma(1)
, and note that for
k
fixed (or
k=1
) this is a
convergent power series function
around zero in
z
.
Finally,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}F_{\rho},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}{1-a_{\rho}(g)\cdot e^{-\langle i^{*}_{\sigma}F_{\rho},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}}=\begin{cases}0,\ \ \text{if}\ a_{\rho}(g)\neq 1,\\
1,\ \ \text{if}\ a_{\rho}(g)=1.\end{cases}

So only
g=id_{G}
contributes a non-zero limit to (
188
), and this contribution is
1
.
Altogether, we get the following result.

Theorem 5.1
.

In the above notations, we have

(189)

\begin{split}\int_{P_{y}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm&=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{e^{\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}[D_{P_{y}(h)}],z\rangle}}{\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle}\\
&=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{e^{\langle(1+y)\cdot i_{\sigma}^{*}c^{\mathbb{T}}_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D_{P})),z\rangle}}{\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle}\cdot e^{\sum_{\rho}h_{\rho}\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},z\rangle}\>.\end{split}

Remark 5.2
.

The left hand side of (
189
) is a
continuous
function in
h
near zero, and for all
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
, resp., in
y
, whereas the right hand side is an
analytic
function in
h
near zero,
and for
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
away from the linear hyperplanes
\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},z\rangle=0
for each ray
\rho\in\sigma(1)
of
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
, resp., in
y
. But then both sides of this equality have to be

analytic functions in
h
near zero and all
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
, resp., in
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, with the corresponding Taylor series around zero converging uniformly on small compact neighborhoods of zero in the variables
h
and
z
(cf. also
[
34
]
[page 27]).

For later use, let us now state the following consequence of Theorem
5.1
(compare also with
[
12
]
[Lem.3.11]).

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
f
be a polynomial function of degree
d
on
M_{\mathbb{R}}
. Then
\int_{P_{y}(h)}f(m)\ dm
is a polynomial function in
h
of degree bounded above by
d+n
.

Proof.

We follow the idea of proof from
[
12
]
[Lem.3.11], adapted to our language. By replacing in (
189
)
z
by
tz
, with
0\neq t\in{\mathbb{R}}
small (so that also

\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},tz\rangle\neq 0
for each ray
\rho\in\sigma(1)
of
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
), and multiplying both sides by
t^{n}
, we get an equality of analytic functions in
t
, with each term on the right hand side also analytic even in zero, since
\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle\neq 0
is homogeneous in
z
of order
n
.
Now taking the Taylor expansion at
t=0
of these two analytic functions, we get for
z
small and away from the linear hyperplanes
\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},z\rangle=0
for each ray
\rho\in\sigma(1)
of
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
, that the assertion holds for
f(m)=\langle m,z\rangle^{k}
, for any given non-negative integer
k
.
This then implies the statement for any polynomial
f(m)
.
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
X=X_{P}
be the toric variety associated to a simple full-dimensional lattice polytope
P\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
, with

D=D_{P}
the corresponding ample divisor. Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a
{\mathbb{T}}
-equivariant
coherent sheaf on
X
. Then, for a polynomial function
f
on
M_{\mathbb{R}}
, the expression

(190)

\sum_{m\in M}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\cdot\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}((1+y)D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{-m}}\right)\cdot f(m)

is a polynomial in
1+y
. Moreover, the value of this polynomial at
0
(i.e., for
y=-1
) is given by

\sum_{m\in M}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\cdot\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{-m}}\right)\cdot f(m).

Proof.

Using the ideas of the above corollary (as in
[
12
]
[Prop.4.1]), the assertion follows from the formula

\begin{split}&\sum_{m\in M}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\cdot\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}((1+y)D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{-m}}\right)\cdot e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\\
&=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{e^{\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}[D_{P_{y}}],z\rangle}}{\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle}\cdot\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}(td_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])),z\rangle\\
&=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma(n)}\frac{e^{\langle(1+y)\cdot i_{\sigma}^{*}c^{\mathbb{T}}_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D_{P})),z\rangle}}{\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle}\cdot\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}(td_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])),z\rangle\>,\end{split}

which can be deduced from (
179
), upon pairing with
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, the expression (
190
) becomes

\sum_{m\in M\cap P_{y}}f(m)

as in
[
12
]
. For the function
f=1
, this further specializes to the classical
Ehrhart polynomial
.
As another special case, for
[{\mathcal{F}}]=mC^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}([O_{\tau}\hookrightarrow X])\in K_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
, (
190
) calculates

\sum_{m\in M\cap{\rm Relint}(E_{y})}f(m)

with
E
the face of
P
corresponding to a cone
\tau\in\Sigma
.

We next explain (in Theorem
5.8
below) a result analogous to Theorem
5.1
for the dilation

Tan(P,v)(h):=(v+C_{v})(h)=v(h)+C_{v}

of the
tangent cone

Tan(P,v)=v+C_{v}
of a vertex
v
of the polytope
P
, with
C_{v}=Cone(P\cap M-v)\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
a full-dimensional simple lattice cone with vertex
0
. The arguments from the case of a full-dimensional lattice polytope apply similarly to the tangent cone, once the approximation of the integral
\int_{{C_{v}}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm
by the corresponding Riemann sum is explained. The corresponding equivariant Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch formula in this setting is (
170
), with
\sigma^{\vee}=C_{v}
and
v
corresponding to the torus fixed point
x_{\sigma}
in the
{\mathbb{T}}
-invariant open affine neighborhood
U_{\sigma}
associated to
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
.

Proposition 5.6
.

With the above notations,
one has that
\sum_{m\in C_{v}\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}
and
\int_{C_{v}}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm
are convergent (locally uniformly) to meromorphic functions in
L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{C}}
, for
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying
-z\in Int(\sigma)
. Moreover,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in k\cdot{C_{v}}(h)\cap M}e^{\langle m,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in{C_{v}}(h)\cap\frac{1}{k}M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\int_{{C_{v}}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm,

with
h\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{r}
a small enough dilation vector, and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
so that
k\cdot C_{v}(h)
is a simple pointed lattice cone in
M_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma(n)
be a smooth
n
-dimensional cone with generators
u_{\sigma,1},\ldots,u_{\sigma,n}
of the rays
\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{n}\in\sigma(1)
, and let
m_{\sigma,1},\ldots,m_{\sigma,n}
be the dual basis of
M=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{N}}\cdot m_{\sigma,i}
. Then

\sum_{m\in C_{v}\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j_{i}=0}^{\infty}(e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle})^{j_{i}}\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}}=\frac{\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}({\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)),z\rangle}{\langle Eu_{X}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle},

with all
\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle<0
for
-z\in Int(\sigma)
, and the corresponding geometric series locally uniformly convergent in these
z
. The last equality follows from (
173
) (for
K=\emptyset
), using the identification
s:Sym_{\mathbb{Q}}(M)\simeq(\Lambda_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Similarly,

\int_{C_{v}}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{-1}{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle},

for
-z\in Int(\sigma)
and convergence as before, with the Lebesgue measure
dm
normalized so that the unit cube in
M\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
has volume
1
(see also
[
29
]
[eq.(8)] and
[
38
]
[Prop.1]). By the multiplicativity of the equivariant Todd class transformation, we get as in the case of a polytope that

\begin{split}\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in k\cdot{C_{v}}(h)\cap M}e^{\langle m,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}&=\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{e^{h_{i}\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}}{1-e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}}\right)\\
&=\lim_{k\to\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{e^{h_{i}\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}\cdot\langle m_{\sigma,i},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}{(1-e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle})\cdot\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}\right)\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{-e^{h_{i}\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}}{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}\\
&=e^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{-1}{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}\\
&=e^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}\cdot\int_{{C_{v}}}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm\\
&=\int_{{C_{v}}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm.\end{split}

For the proof of the case of a simplicial cone by smooth subdivisions, let us remark that the above approximation by Riemann sums for the full cone
C_{v}
holds similarly for the cone
C_{v}^{K}:=C_{v}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in K}F_{i}
with some facets
F_{i}
(
i\in K\subset\Sigma(1)
) removed. More precisely, one gets

\sum_{m\in C^{K}_{v}\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\prod_{i\in\sigma(1)\cap K}\frac{e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}}{1-e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}}\cdot\prod_{i\in\sigma(1)\setminus K}\frac{1}{1-e^{\langle m_{\sigma,i},z\rangle}}=\frac{\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}(T_{0*}^{\mathbb{T}}([V\hookrightarrow X])),z\rangle}{\langle Eu_{X}^{\mathbb{T}}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle},

with the last equality following from (
173
), for
V=X\setminus\bigcup_{i\in K}D_{\rho_{i}}
(with the divisors
D_{\rho_{i}}
corresponding to the divisors of the original polytope
P
). The series on the left hand side are locally uniformly convergent to a meromorphic function in
L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{C}}
, for
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying
-z\in Int(\sigma)
. Moreover, a similar proof yields in this case that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in k\cdot{C^{K}_{v}}(h)\cap M}e^{\langle m,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}=\int_{{C_{v}}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm.

In the general case, let
C_{v}
be as before a full-dimensional (simple) lattice cone with vertex
0
. The assumption that
C_{v}
is simple will not be needed in the following arguments. Consider a refinement
C_{v}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s}C_{i}
of
C_{v}
by smooth full-dimensional lattice cones with vertex
0
, see, e.g.,
[
16
]
[Thm.11.1.9] in the dual context of toric resolutions by fan refinements.
Then, considering each newly introduced facet
F

for only one of the cones it belongs to, we get a disjoint union
C_{v}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{s}C^{K_{i}}_{i}
into smooth full-dimensional lattice cones with vertex
0
and with some facets removed. Let
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
be so that
-z\in Int(\sigma)
, with
\sigma=C_{v}^{\vee}
. Then we also have that
-z\in Int(\sigma_{i})
with
\sigma_{i}=C_{i}^{\vee}
. So we can apply the case of smooth cones to all of the
C_{i}
. Then

\sum_{m\in C_{v}\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\sum_{m\in C^{K_{i}}_{i}\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\right)

is convergent (locally uniformly) to a meromorphic function in
L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{C}}
, for
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying
-z\in Int(\sigma)
. Moreover,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k^{n}}\sum_{m\in k\cdot{C_{v}}(h)\cap M}e^{\langle m,\frac{1}{k}\cdot z\rangle}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\int_{{C_{i}}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm=\int_{{C_{v}}(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm,

with
h\in M_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq{\mathbb{Q}}^{r}
a small enough dilation vector, and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
so that
k\cdot C_{v}(h)
is a full-dimensional pointed lattice cone in
M_{\mathbb{R}}
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

By the above smooth decomposition method, one can get more explicit expressions for the summation appearing in formula (
170
). In the context of the tangent cone
Tan(P,v)
of a simple full-dimensional lattice polytope
P
at a vertex
v
, we also get the identification

(191)

\sum_{m\in C_{v}\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\langle{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)_{x_{\sigma}},z\rangle=\langle\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{m\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right),z\rangle\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}},

for
-z\in Int(\sigma)
. The left hand side is a convergent series for such
z
, whereas the right hand side is a corresponding meromorphic function on
N_{\mathbb{C}}
. Furthermore, still assuming
-z\in Int(\sigma)
, we get more generally, for
\tau\preceq\sigma
corresponding to a face
E
of
P
containing the vertex
v
, a similar interpretation for the following equality:

(192)

\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(C_{v}\cap E)\cap M}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}=\langle T_{0*}^{\mathbb{T}}([O_{\tau}\hookrightarrow X])_{x_{\sigma}},z\rangle=\langle\mathbb{S}\left(\sum_{{\rm Relint}(\sigma^{\vee}\cap\tau^{\perp})\cap M}e^{s(m)}\right),z\rangle\in L^{-1}(\Lambda^{an}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

As a consequence of the above discussion, we can now give a geometric meaning to the localized summands of formula (
189
).

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
v\in P
be a vertex of the full-dimensional simple lattice polytope
P\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
, with tangent cone
Tan(P,v)
, for
C_{v}=Cone(P\cap M-v)\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
a full-dimensional simple lattice cone with vertex
0
. Consider its dilation

Tan(P,v)(h):=(v+C_{v})(h).

Let
(X,D)
be the projective toric variety with ample Cartier divisor
associated to
P
. Denote by
x_{\sigma}\in X
the torus fixed point corresponding to
v\in P
, with associated cone
\sigma=C_{v}^{\vee}\in\Sigma(n)
. Then for
z\in N_{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying
-z\in Int(\sigma)
, we have

(193)

\begin{split}\int_{Tan(P,v)(h)}e^{\langle m,z\rangle}\ dm=\frac{e^{\langle v,z\rangle}}{\langle Eu^{\mathbb{T}}_{X}(x_{\sigma}),z\rangle}\cdot e^{\sum_{\rho\in\sigma(1)}h_{\rho}\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},z\rangle}\>.\end{split}

Proof.

In light of the above proposition, one only has to note that

i_{\sigma}^{*}c_{1}^{\mathbb{T}}({\mathcal{O}}(D))=-c(v)=s(v)\in H^{2}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq M_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

The left hand side of the equality (
193
) is an
analytic function in
h
near zero and
-z\in Int(\sigma)
, resp., in
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover, this integral is locally uniformly convergent in
z
.
The right hand side of (
193
) is an
analytic function in
h
near zero and
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, as well as a meromorphic function in
z
which is holomorphic outside the hyperplanes
\langle i_{\sigma}^{*}F_{\rho},z\rangle=0
for each ray
\rho\in\sigma(1)
.

Back to the context of a projective simplicial toric variety associated to a full-dimensional simple lattice polytope
P\subset M_{\mathbb{R}}
,
