p(t_{i},x_{\rho})\in{\mathbb{Q}}[[t_{1},\dots,t_{n},x_{\rho}\mid\rho\in\Sigma(1)]]
as in (
268
). Then for a polynomial function
f
on
M_{\mathbb{R}}
, we have:

(269)

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{m\in M}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\cdot\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{-m}}\right)\cdot f(m)\>.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Corollary
7.13
by the following calculation:

(270)

\begin{split}&p(\partial_{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial h})\left(\int_{P(h)}f(m)\ dm\right)_{|_{h=0}}\\
&=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\left({\rm mult}(\sigma_{E})\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma_{E}(1)}\frac{\partial}{\partial h_{\rho}}\right)\left(\int_{P(h)}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm\right)_{|_{h=0}}\\
&=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\left(\int_{E(h)}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm\right)_{|_{h=0}}\\
&=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm\>.\end{split}

Here the first equality follows from the definition (
252
) of

p(\partial_{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial h})\left(\int_{P(h)}f(m)\ dm\right)_{|_{h=0}}
and the choice (
268
) of
p(t_{i},x_{\rho})
.
Then the second equality follows from (
261
), and the final equality is just continuity in
h
.
∎

Remark 7.17
.

In the classical case
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
,
this is just Cappell-Shaneson’s recipe for the definition of the differential operators
p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})
, described here geometrically in terms of
the equivariant Todd class
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X):={\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}])\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;\mbox{$\mathbb{Q}$})
(see
[
18
]
[Theorem 2]). More precisely, the differential operator
\mathcal{E}=\sum_{E\preceq P}P_{E}\cdot U_{E}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[[\partial_{1},\dots,\partial_{n}]][U_{F}]
used in
[
43
]
[6.2, page 622] and
[
18
]
[page 888] maps under the homomorphism (
250
) to a presentation of the equivariant Todd class
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>p_{\sigma_{E}}(t_{i})[V_{\sigma_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
. Indeed, the classes
[V_{\sigma_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}}
are the images of the elements
U_{E}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[[\partial_{1},\dots,\partial_{n}]][U_{F}]
used in loc.cit., and the operators
P_{E}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[[\partial_{1},\dots,\partial_{n}]]
are deduced in
[
18
,
43
]
from a representative
T(E)\in{\mathbb{Q}}[[\partial_{1},\dots,\partial_{n}]][U_{F}]
mapping to the equivariant Todd class
{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)
calculated via the equivariant version of Theorem
2.6
. So we can choose
p_{\sigma_{E}}
to be the images of these
P_{E}
in
(\widehat{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
under (
250
). Hence, the presentation

p(t_{i},x_{\rho}):=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\left({\rm mult}(\sigma_{E})\cdot\prod_{\rho\in\sigma_{E}(1)}x_{\rho}\right)\cdot p_{\sigma_{E}}(t_{i})\in{\mathbb{Q}}[[t_{1},\dots,t_{n},x_{\rho}\mid\rho\in\Sigma(1)]]

chosen here is exactly the image of
\mathcal{E}
in
{\mathbb{Q}}[[t_{1},\dots,t_{n}]][[x_{\rho}]]
.

In this case, when
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, the right hand side of (
269
) becomes

\sum_{m\in P\cap M}f(m)
by Example
5.23
.
Specializing further to
f=1
yields then the classical formula (see, e.g.,
[
27
]
[page 112]):

(271)

|P\cap M|=\sum_{E\preceq P}r_{E}\cdot vol(E)\>,

with
r_{E}=p_{\sigma_{E}}(0)
the constant coefficient of
p_{\sigma_{E}}
and

(272)

{\rm td}_{*}(X)=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>r_{E}[V_{\sigma}]\in H^{*}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})

the non-equivariant version of

(273)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{O}}_{X}])=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>p_{\sigma_{E}}(t_{i})[V_{\sigma_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\>.

When
{\mathcal{F}}:=\omega_{X}
, the right hand side of (
269
) becomes

\sum_{m\in{\rm Int}(P)\cap M}f(m)
by Example
5.24
.
Moreover, here we can choose

(274)

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([\omega_{X}])=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>p_{\sigma_{E}}(-t_{i})\cdot(-1)^{\hbox{\rm codim}\,(E)}[V_{\sigma_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})\>,

by Remark
3.26
, with
p_{\sigma_{E}}(t_{i})
as in (
273
).
Specializing further to
f=1
yields the formula

(275)

|{\rm Int}(P)\cap M|=\sum_{E\preceq P}(-1)^{\hbox{\rm codim}\,(E)}\cdot r_{E}\cdot vol(E)\>,

with
r_{E}
as in (
272
).

Example 7.18
.

Here we list further specializations of formula (
269
)
for appropriate choices of
[{\mathcal{F}}]\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)
, resp.,
[{\mathcal{F}}]\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)[y]
, with

{\rm td}_{*}^{\mathbb{T}}([{\mathcal{F}}])=\sum_{E\preceq P}\>p_{\sigma_{E}}(t_{i})[V_{\sigma_{E}}]_{\mathbb{T}}\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})
, resp.,
\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(X;{\mathbb{Q}})[y]

for some elements
p_{\sigma_{E}}(t_{i})\in\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})
, resp.,
\widehat{H}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(pt;{\mathbb{Q}})[y]
.

(1)

For
[{\mathcal{F}}]=mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}(X)\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)[y]
, (
269
) becomes by equation (
230
):

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{E^{\prime\prime}\preceq P}(1+y)^{\dim(E^{\prime\prime})}\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E^{\prime\prime})\cap M}f(m)\>.

(2)

For
[{\mathcal{F}}]=mC_{0}^{\mathbb{T}}([U\hookrightarrow X])\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)
, with
U=X\setminus D_{K}
the open complement of the divisor
D_{K}=\bigcup_{i\in K}D_{i}
and
D_{i}:=D_{\rho_{i}}
, (
269
) becomes by equation (
210
):

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{m\in P^{K}\cap M}f(m)\>.

Here
P^{K}
is the set obtained from
P
by removing the facets
F_{i}
for
i\in K
.
More generally, for
[{\mathcal{F}}]=mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}([U\hookrightarrow X])\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)[y]
, (
269
) becomes by equation (
234
):

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{E^{\prime\prime}\preceq P^{K}}(1+y)^{\dim(E^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E^{\prime\prime})\cap M}f(m)\>.

(3)

For
[{\mathcal{F}}]=[(i_{\sigma})_{*}\omega_{V_{\sigma}}]\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)
, with
V_{\sigma}=V_{\sigma_{E^{\prime}}}
the closure of the orbit of
\sigma
in
X=X_{P}
corresponding to the face
E^{\prime}
of
P
, (
269
) becomes by equation (
214
):

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E^{\prime})\cap M}f(m)\>.

(4)

For
[{\mathcal{F}}]=[(i_{\sigma})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{V_{\sigma}}]\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)
, with
V_{\sigma}=V_{\sigma_{E^{\prime}}}
the closure of the orbit of
\sigma
in
X=X_{P}
corresponding to the face
E^{\prime}
of
P
, (
269
) becomes by equation (
218
):

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{m\in E^{\prime}\cap M}f(m)\>.

More generally, for
[{\mathcal{F}}]=(i_{\sigma})_{*}mC_{y}^{\mathbb{T}}(V_{\sigma})\in K^{\mathbb{T}}_{0}(X)[y]
, (
269
) becomes by equation (
239
):

\sum_{E\preceq P}\>\int_{E}p_{\sigma_{E}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{E^{\prime\prime}\preceq E^{\prime}}(1+y)^{\dim(E^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\sum_{m\in{\rm Relint}(E^{\prime\prime})\cap M}f(m)\>.

∎

7.3.
Generalized Reciprocity for Dedekind Sums via Euler-Maclaurin formulae

We conclude this paper with the following application of formula (
269
), with
D
replaced by
(1+y)D
.

Corollary 7.19
.

In the context of Theorem
7.16
, we have an equality of polynomials in
1+y
,

(276)

\sum_{E_{y}\preceq P_{y}}\>\int_{E_{y}}p_{\sigma_{E_{y}}}(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=\sum_{m\in M}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\cdot\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{O}}_{X}((1+y)D)\otimes{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{-m}}\right)\cdot f(m)\>.

When evaluating these polynomials at zero (i.e., for
y=-1
), one gets the following identity:

(277)

\sum_{v\in P}\left(p_{\sigma_{v}}(\partial_{i})f\right)(0)=\sum_{m\in M}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\cdot\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{i}(X;{\mathcal{F}})_{\chi^{-m}}\right)\cdot f(m).

where the left hand sum is over the vertices of
P
.

Proof.

The right hand sides of the above expressions are calculated in Corollary
5.4
.
For the left hand side of (
276
), note that for a given face
E
of
P
, if
f
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
d_{f}
, and
p(\partial_{i})
is a homogeneous differential operator of degree
d\leq d_{f}
, then

(278)

\int_{E_{y}}p(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm=(1+y)^{\dim(E)+d_{f}-d}\int_{E}p(\partial_{i})f(m)\ dm.

This explains the polynomial behavior of the left hand side of (
276
), and it yields (
277
) upon evaluation at zero (since by letting
y=-1
, all terms on the right hand side of (
278
) vanish except for
\dim(E)=0
and
d=d_{f}
).
∎

Example 7.20
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
in (
277
), one gets by Example
3.18
the following identity:

(279)

\sum_{v\in P}\left(p_{\sigma_{v}}(\partial_{i})f\right)(0)=f(0).

For instance, in the case of lattice polygones, this formula yields generalizations of reciprocity laws for classical
Dedekind sums
(using, e.g., the explicit description of the operators
p_{\sigma_{v}}(\partial_{i})
from
[
18
]
[page 889]). Details will be explained in forthcoming work.
∎
